It sucked having to play WAG while Ilya went out and skated circles around them with prospects who were close but not quite ready for the show. Cartlon and Wiebe had asked Shane to stay home for both games, the Centaurs home and away against the Metros. They worried about the press getting silly ideas almost as much as they worried about Shane's mental state.

It wasn't like there were restraining orders in the settlement. Obviously Shane wasn't going to talk about what happened, not that there was much he could talk about that hadn't already hit the media by his own actions. Shane had agreed to rest for the preseason, but told them this wasn't going to fly when they were fighting for points in the standings, which had made Carlton and Wiebe exchange a glance that suggested they were amused by the insinuation.

Shane was hell bent on advocating for himself. It was one of the more important things he'd had to learn after Montreal and all the changes that came with leaving, specifically firing his mom as his manager. He didn't always voice his needs perfectly, but he certainly tried to be cognizant of situations even as he insisted upon his wants and needs as a player.

Which had led him—them, actually—to this.

It wasn’t a requirement to disclose their relationship, but they’d agreed it wouldn't be fair to blindside the front office if someone figured them out—which had begun looking a lot more likely than when these last few weeks, which boggled Shane's mind to think about. He really thought they'd been obvious, which he’d said to Derek, Luca, and Ilya over lunch last week. Derek had politely insisted that was because straight men were not subtle creatures while Luca and Ilya laughed, the latter patting Shane's cheek in a very patronizing way. Derek was right, though: Even the media had happily leaned into putting a different spin on the rivalry when they’d realized Shane had no plans to tell them anything about Lily besides their shared happiness that Shane was back in Major League Hockey, where he belonged.

Ilya sauntered along beside him with a shit-eating grin. They had gone back and forth about how they wanted to handle things. They could’ve left it to their agent, now that Farah represented them both, but Ilya had a lot of respect for the Centaurs’ organization and Shane trusted him to make this call.

Well, mostly. Maybe just not in the most professional way possible.

“Against my better judgement,” Shane said, the two of them coming to a stop outside Wiebe’s office, I'm going to defer to my captain on this.”

“Ah,” Ilya hummed loftily, “Wise decision.”

“We'll see about that,” Shane glanced sideways at him. “You're a little too excited for me to think you're not going to share the news in the most embarrassing way possible.”

“Sorry, sorry, let me,” he slapped a hand over his mouth and dragged it down, making an exaggerated frown, “There. Now I look like I hate you and they will panic like we have decided this team is not big enough for the two of us.”

The door had opened while Ilya was speaking. Coach Wiebe’s timing could not have been worse.

“Gentlemen?” He stepped out of his office into a very poorly represented situation.

To be fair, their positioning didn't help: Ilya glowering like a Saturday morning cartoon villain at Shane, who had his arms crossed over his chest and an eye roll charged like those laser eyes one of the guys in the superhero show Hayes had forced Ilya to watch last week (Shane had been reviewing film on the other end of the couch, his toes tucked under Ilya's thigh to stay warm so he'd maybe caught a couple things before putting in his earbuds because wow it was violent). Maybe it made sense that Harris hadn't asked them to do their promo together, now that Shane thought about it.

Ilya threw his head back, laughing. “Look what you've done, zaychik.”

Shane’s nose scrunched, earning him the nickname he’d learned about for similar reasons just last week. “Me? How is this my fault?”

Wiebe cleared his throat. “Why don't we take this behind closed doors,” he suggested, almost evenly. There was a little bit of a waver, like he wasn't particularly sure what to make of things. He stepped inside and gestured for them to follow, asking, “Should I call Carlton, or your agen—”

“No,” They said, layered imperfectly over top of one another, “No no no.”

Coach’s shoulders dropped a little. Shane hadn't noticed just how high up they'd been hitched, how much tension he'd been holding. He and Ilya took their seats, leaving Shane to close the door behind them. He hovered a little, eyes darting around the small space. It was nice, Shane supposed, maybe a little dated in the way most arenas were. The only thing that really felt the same was the lighting, yellowy and uncomfortable.

“Shane.” Ilya demurred. “Sit down.” Shane blinked a few times, a little too hard to pass off as normal. Not Montreal, he told himself. Ottawa. How long had he been standing there with his back against the wall? Not long, he hoped.

“Right.” He forced himself to sit in the chair beside Ilya, fingers clamping over his knees reflexively.

“So, gents,” Coach Wiebe began, a note of hesitation returning to his voice as he looked between them, “What can I help you with?”

Ilya leaned forward in Shane's periphery. “Is nothing bad, Coach, promise,” he began, but almost immediately turned to Shane. He must have noticed how tightly Shane was gripping his knees because he frowned. “Is it the conversation—” Shane shook his head microscopically, “Or the room?”

Wiebe's forehead crinkled, probably from trying to figure out what the fuck was going on. Shane was more than a little mortified.

Feeling very stupid and jittery, but cognizant of the fact that Ilya would derail the entire conversation until he spoke up, Shane admitted, “The lights.”

“Ah, okay.” Ilya rose, edging between Shane's knees and the desk, throwing open the office door. “New plan: We will walk and talk.”

Wiebe looked apprehensive as he followed Ilya, who didn't actually do any talking—Shane was pretty sure he wasn't the only one a little nervous about this conversation, albeit for very different reasons—but instead led them to the team lounge, which was unoccupied at this hour. It was a much larger space than the coach’s office, a mix of tables and chairs plus more casual furniture, couches and recliners for guys who needed a space to rest or regroup. Ilya indicated a table on the far side of the room on his way to the fridge against the opposite wall.

He was back in a second, plunking a ginger ale in front of Shane before sliding a coke over to Wiebe. He nudged Shane's shoulder as he sat down, cracking open a can for himself. “Better?”

Shane nodded as Wiebe gingerly lowered himself into a chair as if expecting to fall through it and land on the floor. “Yeah. Thanks.”

“Okay.” Ilya took a deep breath, followed it with a massive exhale, turned his head and began. “I trust you, Coach.”

“Roz, Brandon or Wiebe is fine.” He looked over at Shane who shrugged, before adding, “But thank you for trusting me.”

Ilya barrelled onward, mostly ignoring his statement. “I know everyone is very concerned about the rivalry. Will there be less compete because we are on same team? Will it create tension in the room? Everyone is afraid we will fight or one of us will want out. I know you know this. We,” he gestured to Shane with his flailing hands, “See the way you and rest of coaching staff look at us. Harris, too,” he tacked on like an afterthought.

Privately, Shane had a feeling Ilya really wanted to do promo together. They hadn't in years.

“You guys do go pretty hard,” Coach Wiebe pointed out, his hands folded around his unopened coke on the table. “I know we,” he jerked his chin at Ilya, “Talked about it before Hollzy’s signing, but it's another thing to see day in and day out. I don't think anyone doubts that the rivalry is alive and well.”

“It is,” Shane said succinctly. “But—”

“But,” Ilya continued, giving Shane a very pointed look, a nonverbal me first, “We are happy here. This is where we want to be long term.”

“Agreed.”

“Well, I'm glad to hear that.” Wiebe looked like it, too. “I’m sensing there’s something else you wanted to tell me, though.”

Ilya licked his lips. “There is, ah, one other thing.”

“If this is about playing you two on the same line…”

Shane almost laughed. If only that were their request. “I think it'd be, like, fun to try on the power play, but no,” he rubbed at the skin just below his lips, “ It’s not that,” he finished quietly. “It's, uh, actually about Lily.’

Wiebe leaned in attentively across the pristine white table, the laminate surface glossy under the lights. His patient gaze rested on Shane. “Oh?”

His interest was understandable. The Centaurs organization had shown Shane nothing but the utmost respect. No one from management had brought up Lily or asked for his identity beyond insisting the organization was welcoming of all partners and spouses, and the media had been informed of Shane's personal boundaries so no information had been forthcoming.

“Yes.”

Ilya's thigh pressed insistently against Shane's under the table. Shane tipped his head sideways, a subtle go on with it implied with the gesture.

“It is not a secret, exactly,” Ilya told Wiebe, “But we are not telling anyone either.”

“Of course,” Wiebe was all business, professional to a fault like Ilya had suggested he would be. “Shane doesn't have to tell anyone about his relationship unless there’s a contractual obligation to disclose. If someone is pressuring you about it—” He eyed Shane with concern, but Shane was already speaking over him.

“No, that's not—”

“You are not understanding, I think,” Ilya cut Shane off, scratching the back of his head, “So I will be blunt: I am Shane’s Lily.”

Shane could feel the heat that bloomed on his cheeks as well as the lobes of his ears, but Ilya was reaching over to gently pry his fingers off the condensating can of ginger ale, folding his slightly larger hand over Shane's in a show of solidarity.

Swallowing hard, he forced himself to add, “ We wanted you to hear it from us. We weren't really sure if it would be obvious in camp. We're not like, hiding it, you know?”

Wiebe did not know, Shane decided. Wiebe blinked kind of rapidly, looking back and forth between them, appearing rather gobsmacked. “You’re him,” he said slowly in Ilya's direction. “Really.” Now he was looking around the room. “This isn’t some—Harris didn't plant a camera somewhere, did he—”

“He is,” Shane confirmed, hating the idea of anyone joking about it. “Serious, I mean. He is.”

Ilya preened. “Ilya, Lily. Lily, Ilya. Is very close.” He winked.

Shane groaned.

“What?”

“The winking. Really?”

The levity seemed to snap Wiebe out of his surprise long enough to lock back into the conversation.

“This does not need to be so serious. I know, is a big shock for Coach but is not sad news. We are together now and we are going to bring cup to Ottawa, together,” he squeezed their linked hands before returning to Wiebe. “But also someone in management should probably know before the rest of the world catches on.”

“We won't let it impact our play.”

“I'm not worried about that,” Wiebe insisted, shaking his head. His features, younger and less grizzled than most of his peers, were schooled into something serious but no less moved, if the warmth in his eyes was any indication. At least, Shane hoped it was. “But I do think we should tell Carlton.” His lips thinned. “I don't think it'll come to this but I want to make sure we're in your corner if the League tries anything.”

“Fine,” Ilya said, because he and Shane had figured Wiebe would send their news up the chain. “Is not really a secret, anyway. We will not deny when people figure it out, but we do not want it leaked to press. Whole world will find out eventually,” Ilya’s expression was firm. “But we do not owe them anything.”

Chapter 10

Chapter Notes

Double update this week. Homeopener today, Halloween Part I (of III) tomorrow.

PS: thank you all for your lovely comments, kudos, and for reading my silly little stories. It means a lot to be able to share them! ❤️

We do not owe them anything, Ilya had said. Shane clung to it like a mantra through the weekend and the days after, as he showered and dressed for their first game of the season. I don't owe them anything, he told himself. I don't owe them anything.

It was a good mantra, but that didn't mean it would survive real life application.

Forty-five minutes before their homeopener, Harris found Shane in the dressing room. He had a headset on and clipboard in hand, looking business apologetic. At least he'd let Shane finish pulling up his fucking jock before talking. “Both broadcasts asked to mic you up for the game. TSN asked for a twenty second interview during TV timeout in the first—”

“And Sportsnet wants an intermission spot,” Shane interrupted, guessing.

“And post game. There was also some talk of wanting to get a commercial break with your parents, but I relayed your veto on that.”

“Jesus Christ,” he muttered, along with a belated, “Thanks.”

“Boo,” Ilya yelled from across the room, sharp eyes on the back of Harris’ denim vest. It had a massive Centaurs logo embroidered on the back of it, but the pride version, loud and rainbow. Ilya told Shane that he wore it every game day. “Save some attention for the rest of us.”

“You're such an asshole,” Shane said, but he could feel his shoulders drop a little.

Harris was biting his lip, looking a little nervous as he peeked at Ilya over his shoulder. “I know you just want to play, but—”

“You're just doing your job, Harris,” Shane interrupted evenly, unwilling to let himself get upset this close to gametime. “S’okay. I'll do it.”

He had not missed this portion of playing in this particular league. It was nice to just go out and work hard without every muttered swear caught on a hot mic, not that Shane swore more than most guys, but it did make him self conscious.

“You should clip microphone to my back when no one is looking,” Ilya told Shane when Harris left to retrieve the little clip on he'd be expected to wear for the game.

“Please don't,” Troy pleaded, walking to his stall in a towel, which he promptly dropped in favor of pulling on his base layers. Shane had been surprised to learn that Troy had quietly come out to the people he cared about over the course of the last season and outright shocked to find out he and Harris were in a relationship, which the team had accepted easily. “He’s been so stressed about your return going well and trying not to piss you off with all their asks. I had to sleep in the guest room last night because he's been talking about work in his sleep.”

“Sounds like you are not properly relieving his stress,” Ilya said suggestively.

“Fuck off,” Troy replied, lobbing a roll of tape at him.

Shane did him one better, taking the folded and balled up bundle that was his socks and throwing them at Ilya's head. They bounced off his cheek and onto the floor, rolling to a stop between them.

The rest of the room went still and silent as Ilya looked down at them, folding forward to hook a finger inside the bundle and lift them up. He examined them silently before throwing them back in Shane's direction. “The fuck are you all staring at?” He demanded, when the socks had sailed through the air, returning to Shane's outstretched hand.

The guys looked away, some of them murmuring to each other too softly to be properly heard. Shane was still looking at Ilya who jerked his head toward the guys, but it was impossible to tell what he wanted Shane to do about it.

Wyatt, the first to finish dressing, slapped his helmet on, cage up so his face was still visible and ambled toward the door leading out to the ice. He turned back to survey the room. “You guys do know they agreed to play here, together, right? They're not going to murder each other over socks.”

Shane laughed, unable to help himself. He had maybe possibly had a small meltdown about finding dirty, mismatched socks in their bedroom like, last week? Growing pains of learning to live together and all that. His mom had laughed at him for it when he'd called her to blow off steam.

He looked up from the floor to find Ilya smiling at him, like he knew just what Shane was thinking. He said, “I don't know, he will probably want to murder me when I beat him in scoring, again.”

“Old habits die hard, huh,” Shane quipped back, heatless. “You had three years to be the best. My turn now.”

“Oh, you think?” Ilya grinned. “Want to make a bet, Hollander? Whoever scores most goals this month dresses as the other at my Halloween party on thirty-first.”

Heads around the room rocketed upward.

“You're throwing the Halloween Bash?” Dykstra blurted, manic. “You barely even show up at team parties.”

LaPoint followed it up with a slightly more tasteful, “No fuckin way, party at Cap’s house?”

Even Bood, who loved hosting, was all in. “Hollzy, you gotta agree, I gotta see this with my own two eyeballs.”

It was funny. They'd only discussed the concept of a Halloween party in passing, but Ilya had immediately latched onto it.

This house has perfect views and lots of room for hosting, their realtor had said, showing them the fifth property they'd toured that day in late August. Ilya had floated room to room, admiring and admonishing finishes while Shane stood directly in the center of each, eyeing corners and outlets and detailing likes and dislikes and potential issues in his notes app. Her voice had caught both their attention for different reasons.

We could host Halloween party, Ilya had said, excited in a way he hadn't been in the other houses they'd seen. Kitchen is massive and you liked backyard, big patio and room for your fire pit.

Shane, meanwhile, had pocketed his phone and walked from the kitchen back to the empty living room, eyeing the fireplace and the warm, dark hardwood flooring, turning around to take in the sliding glass door between the two that led to the massive deck and entertaining space outside. The configuration was different from his cottage but it had similar bones: warm wood, high ceilings, lots of open spaces with smaller, more intimate nooks to hide away in. It wasn't perfect, but it would be with a little work.

The rest was just details.

Details like both their names being listed on the deed to the property, the pair of them opting out of practice to sit side by side with Shane’s real estate lawyer, initialing page after page of the packet that made the property theirs for good. His mom had wondered if they were maybe moving a little too fast, especially given the sock fight that had happened mere days before they closed on it, but that was just details, too. Shane refused to do things by half where Ilya was involved.

So: Yes. The Halloween party at their house, not that the team knew that yet.

“Okay Rozanov,” Shane said, extending a hand. “You're on.”

They shook on it. Ilya's gaze could have powered the sun, it was that hot.


—


The ceremony of a team’s homeopener was overwhelming, tedious, and ultimately, necessary. It was all about riling up the crowd, getting them amped up and ready for the year ahead, giving the fans a first look at the roster, giving them faces to go with the names printed on the backs of their jerseys.

This year, Centaurs fans had a lot to be excited about.

Shane didn't want to get too excited alongside them, that would only make him nervous. For that reason, he'd tuned out the announcer after his name had been called, launching himself out of the red and black hoof-shaped arch at the end of the tunnel that shot fire and whatever other pyrotechnics he could smell in the air, waving and looking around without really seeing or hearing the crowd’s reaction. They'd said the same thing for him that they did for everyone: From Ottawa, Ontario, number twenty-four, Shane Hollander.

But as the numbers crept higher, it was impossible to tune out the electric quality of the fans. They were like a live wire, making his teeth vibrate. Troy, standing to his left, leaned over and said behind a glove, “Man are they glad to see you,” and Shane smiled down at the camera operator who did a slow-roll down the row of players, his camera tilted up at their faces.

At least he hoped he did.

“Hopefully I don't let them down,” Shane returned.

“You fuckin’ kidding me? Câlisse, brother, you're the prodigal son returned home.” Chouinard, no, Chewie, he’d insisted, shoved up against Shane's right shoulder, knocked the sides of their helmets together.

“I just want to play,” he emphasized, words tucked mostly behind his glove, head turned toward Chewie this time.

It made his eyes snag on his stick tape, on the -ily peeking out behind his glove, the i dotted with a little heart, which had been enough to make Ilya’s eyes go soft when he'd seen it before sending him down the tunnel and it to the crowd. Not that Shane was any better, because he'd seen Jane written on Ilya's stick in what might be the worst handwriting he had ever seen and wanted to wrap him up in an embrace. They'd settled for a more appropriate helmet bump and Ilya's traditional I love you which Shane mouthed back, and now he was here between number seventeen, Troy Barrett, and number twenty-six, Nick Chouinard.

Alternates and captains were introduced last, despite their jersey number. By the time the announcer got to From Moscow, Russia, pausing to denote emphasis, Your captain, Ilya Rozanov, the fans were on their feet, palms slapping the glass, the boards rattling like they couldn't be contained.

Ilya gave the crowd a jaunty salute, spinning wide, as if giving each and every one of them a piece of his attention. If the arena was a kingdom, Ilya was its king, its ruler, the one in charge. The jittery feeling in Shane’s belly settled unexpectedly as he realized how it was. It hadn't really sunk in that he didn't have to be The Guy, the one the fans and media looked to as the pulse of the franchise until this very moment. He could lean on his partner, lover (eww), boyfriend captain for that.

Ilya met his eyes as he swung around, the C ever-prominent on his chest catching in the sway of the spotlights roving the ice from overhead. He winked, smile so bright it was like a camera flash.

Shane felt his shoulders relax as the arena singer belted out the national anthems and the questions he (and everyone else clued into North American hockey) asked of himself fell away.

His mind emptied. The mantra he'd recalled for days returned to the forefront of his thoughts. It had served its purpose, got him through the waiting. It was still relevant, but he had absorbed it into himself. He didn't need it anymore.

Let them ask, he thought. Let them see. His pride was a quiet, confident thing, brought on by hard work and passion and tempered by adversity.

The lights came up and the goal horn blared. The boys shook themselves out and swarmed as the net was replaced, eager to dig in.

All Shane had to do was play his game.


—


His first shift came forty seconds in. Both teams had sent their first line out for the opening draw, Ilya leaning in and chirping the Buffalo centre who swore a hearty fuck off just in time to lose the faceoff, as was his destiny. After, Wiebe had thrown the fourth line out against their opponents’ second, which was a ballsy move but one that worked against a rebuilding team, effectively shutting them down.

Then, Coach tapped Shane. He threw a leg over the boards, ready to swap with Dillon when he approached. Haas had already come on and Derek wasn’t far behind. It wasn't the line Shane was expecting to wind up on. Sure, playing with Derek was a no brainer. They had insane chemistry, which was even more apparent after camp.

But Luca? Luca was unexpected. Ilya had fought Wiebe on it, which he'd confessed guiltily when the lines were announced. Shane understood the gatekeeping, even if he was logical enough to understand that opening night lines rarely made it to playoffs without a dozen or more shakeups. Luca wasn't Ilya, but Shane could see the foundations, could tell just how special this kid would be in the future.

He needed to be tested and pushed, though. Not through words or chiding, which were Ilya's wheelhouse, but through action. What Shane knew, but Ilya didn't, was that Derek also thrived in a situation where he was forced to keep up, but he was like magic when he could push someone else to do it too, saying keep up, follow me, if I can do it so can you, in actions, rather than words.

They had been good in their final practice, the passes sharp and sense of each other tight. Now it was time to dial that up another notch.

Shane heard the roar of the crowd when his blades touched down, but didn't let himself look away from the game. He'd had time to savor this as much as he'd planned to already so he swiveled his head, saw Luca battling with a defenseman at their blue line, and got to work.

The added pressure let Luca play the puck back to Chewie who rang it around behind Hazy over to Derek, who streaked off like a bullet, Buffalo's defense halfway through a change but quick to reset. Luca took off with speed, getting around his man, giving Derek a good passing lane, the two of them orchestrating a chance on goal without Shane doing much more than a pylon, a bright and shiny distraction.

Which to be fair, he kind of was, at least until it was time to take the offensive zone faceoff. The kid across from him was young. He swallowed hard and didn't meet Shane's eyes for whatever reason (nerves or possibly something else), but hunkered down, ready to fight for the puck.

One of the things Shane had been most worried about was complacency in the faceoff circle, so once training camp hit, he and Ilya had spent time after practice making sure Shane hadn't lost a step after years of playing less experienced (less talented, Ilya insisted) opponents.

He won the face off clean, slapped the puck back to Luca who cycled it, their offense fanning out, defensemen taking each side of the point and collapsing in with precision and patience. Shane began to cut toward the middle but stopped, saw the lane before it opened, saw one of Buffalo's defensemen overcommit.

There was no need to beg for the feed, no time to tap his stick. All of it was fractional, pieces of a handful of seconds. Faceoff, wheel, pass, pass, shot, thnnk, silence.

A visceral sound, something beyond words, something that was just emotion, loud and unfiltered and uncontrollable broke that silence. It bubbled up from beneath the ice, maybe, through the knee he'd taken to clap the puck, up his spine and out his mouth, down his arms, through his stick held up and out at the apex of the shot, blade not quite level with the crossbar and beyond.

Time caught up to the rest of them. The goal horn blared. Shane pushed up and off his knee, back upright. He pointed to Derek who was already barreling over to him as whatever goal song played overhead, then pushed toward him to intercept the hug.

“Fuckin’ bomb Hollzy!” He roared far too loudly in Shane's face, jittery and excited. He squeezed Shane tight, then let up to welcome the rest of their line into their huddle of bodies.

“That's how you fucking do it boys!” Young cheered.

“They clocked it at ninety miles an hour,” Chewie told them. He must have looked up at the jumbotron.

“Not my fastest,” Shane admitted. “Guess I've got some work to do.”

“Oh my God,” one of them muttered behind him. Luca, maybe. He peeled off from the group to grab the puck from the goalie to give to the bench, which Shane ought to be skating toward, now that he thought about it.

He spun around, and realized that the entire Centaurs bench was on its feet, sticks bashing the boards like someone had won a fight or something. He let himself look around, realized the crowd was also on its feet and chanting his name.

Derek threw an arm around him again, steering them toward the bench as a unit. “How you feeling, buddy?” He asked.

Shane sniffed, swiping back the swift insurgence of tears that blurred his vision. “Grateful,” he said thickly.

He bumped fists with his teammates, feeling the swat of sticks on his ass and legs through the padding. There were a couple atta boy’s and let's fucking go’s, encouragement he drifted through. When the referees signaled to center ice, wanting play to continue, Shane arced smoothly back toward the bench.

Ilya came over the boards. He attempted to go around, but nope, Ilya was his mirror and contact was mild, but unavoidable. Shane didn't shrink away from it, thrilled to realize he felt no fear or shame. Ilya was incandescent, gloved hands coming up to grab his shoulders as he knocked their helmets together.

“Beautiful goal, Hollander,” he said, his pouty lips curved upward in a smile that was boyish and genuine.

He couldn't help but grin up at him. “Try to keep up, Rozanov.”

Ilya raised his eyebrows, mouthing a silent little wow and shaking his head, before reaching for Shane's collar and leaning in and there, there was the arrival of his signature flirty, playful grin. He bent down, bushy curls tickling Shane's face as he addressed the microphone there. “Hello everyone watching this at home,” he said into Shane's chest, “You are hearing it here first: Rivalry is not dead.”

“Get off me,” Shane squawked, laughing. “You're losing the bet.”

“I will score hat trick just for you,” Ilya called as he skated away, blowing a kiss over his shoulder.

“Menace,” commented Shane to himself—and to the public, he supposed, because he'd forgotten for a moment that he was micced up.


—


They won the game. How could they not? Buffalo put up a decent fight, they were better than Shane remembered, but Ottawa was a freight train with speed, fucking unstoppable. Shane fulfilled his obligations, and the media behaved themselves—probably under pain of Harris’ ire.

Probably also because they'd heard his comments on the game mic. He'd given them plenty of material to work from.

One reporter, whom Ilya had pointed out to him when he'd signed as being the most senior and trusted among them, waited until the rest of them had asked about the game and the rivalry and the moments that would no doubt be talked about to death and back because Shane still had it (he'd never lost it, even). Some of the best guys who had made the trip from Buffalo had already moved on to Wyatt, who'd gotten an impressive shutout.

Davey—the reporter—looked to Harris, which was a clue in and of itself, which made Shane tense. He seemed to clock it. “Shane,” he began almost gently, “No pressure to answer this one, but was Lily watching?”

The rest of the reporters who had yet to flock around Wyatt leaned back in, phones creeping back toward Shane's face. He ignored them in favor of meeting the reporter's eyes versus something potentially revealing, like looking at Ilya. It was only game one. There would be time for that.

“He was,” Shane smiled.

Chapter 11

October was a month for figuring things out. It was for learning how to manage mismatching pregame routines with someone you lived with and compromising about whose car would be taken to the rink (even if both cars were technically Ilya’s). It was about being obvious in plain sight and getting disbelieving looks from the handful of people who knew. It became an exercise in laughing like idiots when Shane tried to sneak his bag into Ilya’s room in Detroit on their first road trip only to get caught in the elevator by Coach Wiebe because how in all those years prior did they avoid being found out?

By the middle of October, they had moved house—easy for Shane, easier for Ilya with Shane and his parents’ involvement, though there were still jobs to be done (Shane wanted a rink in the basement to go with the gym but had compromised with Ilya’s request for actual ice: over the summer they’d install a small plastic rink for stickwork but the more pressing concern was an outdoor rink in the backyard), had the two-day road trip in which Wiebe had caught them, and allowed the media to become deeply invested in their bet. It caused Ilya to really ham it up, a phrase Shane learned he despised(You compare me to pig? Me? Take that back. I am sexy ferocious bear, not pig). By the end of October they were neck and neck, fighting for ice time, pushing each other in a way that Shane had sorely missed.

He couldn't remember the last time he had felt this voracious when it came to playing. It had been a long time.

When it became clear they could end the month with the same number of goals, tiebreaker conditions were established. Points would be the decision-maker, followed by faceoff percentage if they somehow managed to score the same number of points.

In the end, their conditions wouldn't be necessary. On the night of October twenty-ninth, with no more games to play, Shane had nine goals and twelve assists to Ilya’s ten and eight. Ilya had managed to have a two goal night to end the month and had joyfully informed the press at the optional morning skate on October thirtieth that he couldn't wait to see Shane wear his jersey, that there would be ‘so many pictures.’

On the morning of Halloween, just like every other morning since the season began, they woke up together.

Usually Shane rising to brush his teeth was what woke Ilya, but this morning Ilya was most of the way awake, dozy and sweet and cuddled up behind Shane, tucked into the inverted comma of his body, lips pressed his shoulder, eyelashes tickling bare skin with every blink. The scent of morning breath wasn't great, but Shane had managed to tilt his head just right to avoid it. He was comfortable and didn’t want to move.

Being with Ilya like this made his brain quiet in a good way. It was kind of like having a weighted blanket halfway draped over him, literally and otherwise. He relaxed back into Ilya’s embrace.

“No run this morning?”

“Mm,” Shane shrugged. “Day off.”

“I did not know you took those,” Ilya rumbled.

“You’re too comfy,” he slurred.

He wasn’t sure how long he’d drifted back off for—Ilya really was like a sedative, warm and heavy. Skin on skin contact with him was like a drug. When next Shane knew, Ilya was pulling away very carefully, tucking the blanket in around him to prevent the cold air of the room from touching his skin.

“Bood will be here soon with grill and slow cookers,” Ilya said apologetically. “It is whole day production.”

“I’ll get up,” Shane decided, though he hadn’t opened his eyes yet. “What time is it?”

“Nine o’clock,” Lips pressed into his hair. “You still have time if you want to sleep.”

Shane both did and didn’t. On one hand, he was still warm and comfortable, but that would change once Ilya got up and the overwhelming crush of his thoughts pressed in to fill the void. Today was a busy day. There was a lot to be done: coolers to fill and decorations to put up, food to plate and arrange from the chef they’d hired to do the sides and appetizers that took up space in every refrigerator: kitchen, basement wet bar, garage...

Yeah, no. Shane was definitely getting up.

“You’ll hog all the hot water,” he said instead of any of the thoughts in his head, flopping onto his back in anticipation of Ilya’s response.

He wasn’t disappointed.

“What are you talking about?” Ilya whirled back around, hands flying out in front of himself. “Is endless hot water! You wouldn’t shut up about it. ‘We need tankless water heater, Ilya,’” he whined in a not quite parody of Shane. “‘I want it installed before we move, Ilya. I am printsessa, Ilya.’”

“I know I definitely didn’t say that last one,” Shane deadpanned, throwing back the covers and lowering his socked feet to the floor.

“Is no less true,” Ilya insisted. “I am going to brush my teeth now so I can kiss you without you complaining. Then we are going to shower—and only shower, pervert, you will not seduce me—” Shane walked by him and got his ass smacked for the trouble.

“It’s not my fault if you can’t resist me,” he pointed out, looking over his shoulder at Ilya.

Ilya shook his head, following him into their massive master bath. He placed a kiss on Shane’s shoulder as he went to his sink, the one on the far wall, closer to the water closet. “I love this,” he murmured.

A molten sort of warmth filled Shane’s belly despite the goosebumps on his arms from the cool air. He had very specific feelings about thermostats and sleeping temperatures, which Ilya indulged. “Me too. Sometimes it’s like, I don’t know…”

“A dream,” Ilya said around his toothbrush, foamy toothpaste dotting his lower lip. His gaze softened. “Da. Yes, I agree.”

—

True to Ilya’s word, Zane Boodram arrived around ten, which wouldn’t have been an issue if Ilya hadn’t immediately dropped to his knees in the shower, more or less seducing himself. Shane was pretty sure he was going to have to do a lube check to make sure Ilya didn’t have any hidden in random places—again. His dad had found some in the living room hutch that ran behind the couch and it had taken days for Shane to look at it without blushing like a guilty teenager caught out after curfew. Or, at least, that's how he imagined it felt.

Ilya bounded to the door when the bell rang, going outside barefoot without his slides and yelping when his feet touched cold pavement. It was barely five degrees. Shane shook his head and went on with his morning, making his usual coffee. Feeling festive, he hit it with a little bit of cinnamon on top, then put the tiny glass jar back into the spice rack.

By then, Bood was following Ilya inside, the two of them loaded up with bags over their arms and a massive crockpot cradled in each of them.

“Oh shit, hey Hollzy,” Bood greeted him, carefully resting his crockpot on the counter where Ilya indicated despite his surprise. “I didn’t see your car out front.”

“It’s in the garage,” Shane shrugged. It wasn't technically his car at all, but apparently none of their teammates had ever seen Ilya drive it, so Bood could be forgiven for the assumption. “Need any help bringing things in?”

“Nah, we should be good. Just need Roz to open the gate to the back so I can back the trailer in. Most of the meat’s already smoking and the rest is fine to stay marinating in the cooler until I grill it tonight."

“You worry about drink coolers, yes?” Ilya gave him a knowing look. It was his I know you’re particular and have a plan so do your plan look, which currently doubled as a do not worry about our teammate taking over our backyard, I won’t let him fuck with your patio setup look.

Shane had already pulled out his phone and was trying to figure out if a mobile trailer with an in-use meat smoker was safe, much less legal. Did Bood need some sort of license for it? Ontario had all sorts of rules about what qualified as a food truck…

He forced himself to pocket his phone, shaking his head. Ilya was right. He was definitely better off working inside, possibly in the garage where he couldn't see what was happening in the backyard through the windows. “Okay.”

The pair went out through the garage, chatting about music or something, leaving Shane to drain his coffee and set about loading up beer and drinks in the coolers he'd strategically place inside and out. There was a spot on the deck next to the wet sink that was perfect for a bar, but Shane would wait until Ilya and Zane had finished the barbecue set up to bring out the bottles.

Organizing the drinks was a mindless, pleasant task, one that gave Shane a ton of satisfaction. The cans and bottles were lined up in perfect little rows, organized by size, shape, and color. He tucked a sealed plastic bag with foam koozies in the small gap between the final row of drinks and the insulated wall to finish things off, a hosting tip his mom had taught him one Canada Day weekend long, long ago.

Once they were all loaded up, the coolers staying inside stationed throughout the kitchen and living room, and the majority lined up near the back door to go out in the backyard, Shane brought the final one, full of drink boxes and small water bottles for trick or treaters, to the front hall and turned his attention to the Halloween candy Ilya had bought.

There was a lot of it. They lived in a nice neighborhood, the kind parents would drive their kids to for more ‘premium’ trick-or-treating. Ilya, who had spent enough time in affluent Boston neighborhoods to be aware of the concept, had already gone to the bank for money which he usually rolled up and rubberbanded to full sized candy bars.

Shane had vetoed that presentation on account of them not being drug dealers, and ordered some Halloween gift bags, which he stuffed with a pack of hockey cards, a candy bar, and a twenty dollar bill, which was left flat and nicely pressed. He brought a couple boxes full of them to the door and left them by the cooler, along with another plastic bag with a handful of permanent markers for the inevitable autographs they would be asked to sign.

They had discussed how handing out bags to trick or treaters would work throughout the party and agreed that it would be the responsibility of whomever was closest to the door, and Shane was pretty sure Harris was going to have the time of his life recording content, even on what was supposed to be his night off.

He had just pulled the box of pumpkin shaped lanterns that would line the front steps out of the hall closet when he heard footsteps behind him. They were hesitant and uneven, so definitely not Ilya's, tracing a path around the kitchen, pantry door opening and closing before they continued on.

“Everything okay?” He set the box down and headed back toward the kitchen to find Boodram looking into the cabinets under the sink.

Bood straightened, turning to face Shane. “Just looking for some rags, Roz wants to wipe down the adirondack chairs by the fire pit.”

“Laundry room.” He jerked his head in that direction. “Second door on the left, check the cabinets over the sink.” He stopped himself from adding something awkward like I think that’s where I saw them, or that’s where I would have put them. Shane had been the one to put them there, and even though it had been years since he’d been forced to hide on purpose, that instinct had never completely left.

“Thanks, man.” Bood stopped by the fridge, pulling out a bottle of water from the door and guzzling half of it. “I don’t think he’d admit to it, but I think he’s nervous. This is the first time he’s hosted a team gathering.”

Shane leaned against the counter. This he could play dumb about for Ilya's sake. “Really? He likes parties.”

“Yeah, but he was—don’t get me wrong, I love Roz, we all do, but he was pretty hands off, y’know? Like great in the room and obviously on the ice, but the only guy who ever really went to his place was Wyatt because goalie privilege or whatever. I’d never been to his condo.”

“It was fine,” he offered.

“You’ve been there?”

“Yeah. It’s not a big deal.” Shrugging, Shane followed Bood down the hall. “I didn’t exactly host events when I was Montreal’s captain, either,” he said, ignoring the unpleasant twist in his belly at the mention. “Pike hosted all of them.”

“You had your reasons,” Bood tried, aiming for tactfulness.

Shane forced himself not to exhale too loudly, drew it out through his nostrils, accepting that he was probably one of those reasons for Ilya, and even though he couldn’t change the past, he was here now. “I’m sure Roz does, too.”

Bood inclined his head, reaching for the door handle with the one not wrapped around his water bottle. “Touché, Hollzy.”

The laundry room had dark tiled floors and white cabinets that matched the washer and dryer. Bood looked around at the setup, whistling as he took in the rod by the window with a row of neat suit bags hanging over it, dry cleaning tickets secured around the hangars to indicate they were ready to wear. A clean load of laundry—Ilya’s, because Shane always folded his clothing the minute the dryer went off—sat atop the counter beside it.

“I think Cassie—my wife,” he explained, even though Shane hadn’t forgotten meeting her at Bood’s pre-season barbecue the month before, “Has this exact laundry room on a pinterest board somewhere,” he remarked, begrudgingly impressed. “Gotta admit I’m glad she’s on baby duty tonight or she’d spend the whole night trying to convince me we need to renovate with an infant.”

“Well,” Shane joined him in the small room and indicated the basket of unfolded laundry, “It can’t be all that if he doesn’t bother folding.” They both chuckled.

“Yeah, but,” Zane gave him room, poking his head out into the hall as he lowered his voice. “The guys and I figured that was probably something his girl did when she came around. Not that he talks about her.” He paused, sending Shane a wordless glance asking to be proven wrong.

Shane shrugged, which he was pretty sure was the correct response.

“He made this comment about a Montreal girl in the playoffs last year, Jane. We knew he got some, obviously,” Bood rolled his eyes goodnaturedly, “But that was always casual.” He made a little shimmy with his shoulders. “Now he’s got a house. He’s more settled and shit. Some of the guys were talking and we were kind of hoping she’d make an appearance tonight, you know?”

“T-totally,” Shane said.

“To be fair, they ask about your guy, too. Not like, speculation,” Bood was quick to add, “Just that we’d be chill with it, if you brought him around. We’re not really on that dudebro shit like some other teams. Hopefully that’s noticeable.”

Nodding, Shane opened the cupboard and pressed up onto his toes to grab the cleaning rags on the top shelf over the sink. “Yeah, it’s… yeah. I appreciate that. He does, too.”

“Cool. And, I hope you’re not bending over backwards for Roz or anything to smooth things over, yeah? I know it’s like, contentious, but—”

“It’s not,” Shane blurted. “It’s—we’re just competitive.” He sighed. It would be so easy to just say it, but he’d promised Ilya he’d try to make sure they could do it together as a unit, let him get a read on the room. “I’ll, um, bring these out to him,” he decided, ejecting himself from the conversation.

“Oh, um, okay.” Boodram stayed where he was, probably aware of just how awkward Shane was feeling. He seemed like the perpetual dad type, kind of like Hayden: easygoing and quick to catch the vibe, able to roll with it even when things got weird. “I’m gonna take a leak. Meet you guys out back.”

“Okay,” Shane threw over his shoulder. He’d managed to shove his feet into his slides when he heard the slosh-thud of a half-full bottle of water—hopefully closed—hit the hardwood.

Chapter 12: Interlude: Zane Boodram

Chapter Notes

Shoutout to those of you who recognized that water bottle drop for what it was. Anyway, here's Zane Boodram and the Hollanov emotional rollercoaster.

Zane Boodram was the first guy to admit he had a damn good life, all things considered. He had a wife he fucking adored, the cutest fucking baby on the planet, and got paid unfathomable amounts of money to play a sport he fucking loved. He’d been in Ottawa for years now, since he’d been drafted. He had roots and shit, especially now that he and Cassie were parents. He wanted to stay here forever. Raise his kid, maybe have another one in a year or two if Cassie was okay with it. He was happy.

Which was why he could see it when guys weren’t. He could tell when it wasn’t the yips or garden variety goalie weirdness that had Hazy worked up but something at home, maybe something his wife was going through while they were on the road that he couldn’t properly support her through on FaceTime. He knew when one of the kids on defense had gone and fallen for a fling of the month even though they’d said it wasn’t that serious and needed some time alone to work through it. His mama had always said he had a lot of empathy.

So he’d known, beyond any shadow of a doubt, that Ilya Rozanov was miserable in Ottawa.

Zane had been so fucking hype in that first training camp after Roz had signed. The team was a massive heap of dog shit, but in signing him they’d acquired a backbone that wouldn’t crumble under the slightest adversity, leaving Zane alone with a bunch of second-rate call ups from the minors and a goalie with a glass groin who could give you a save but would need to go on IR to recover from it.

Roz had been step one, and he’d shown up to camp sluggish and disinterested, yet completely above scrutiny. It had taken him until December that first year, the 2018-2019 season, to lock in and be a problem for other teams to play against. In the meantime, he’d fought with almost everyone: they didn’t try hard enough (funny coming from him but not like anyone could actually call him out on it), they weren’t good enough, and what the fuck was this organization doing? They’d promised him if he signed that they’d be a cup contender within two years so shit or get off the pot already.

Of all the people he’d beefed with, Zane hadn’t been one of them. He’d looked Zane up and down on the powerplay three days before Christmas and said, smarmy and assholish, Try to keep up this time, kid, and Zane had met his cocky little grin, nodded, and proceeded to do it.

They’d been linemates, more or less, ever since. Come coaching changes and temporary roster shakeups, they were damn good together.

They’d been even better once Roz woke up and realized he had the C on his sweater. Zane’s voice had weight because it was one of the more veteran ones in the room, but Roz was the kind of guy you listened to whether you’d been in the show for twelve minutes or twelve years. He was the kind of guy you wanted to get in good with. Once you got through the outer shell of asshole, it was too late. You’d die for him if you could. He was a good guy, somewhere deep down.

In all the years since, Zane had been able to figure out why Roz took the payday from Ottawa when it was clear they’d needed to do a lot of work to make the playoffs, much less contend for a cup. The last three seasons had seen them completely overhaul the team inside and out: acquiring Roz on in free agency in 2018, new GM and coach in 2019, trading for Barrett and Hayes in 2020, polishing up Holmberg, LaPointe, and Young into everyday MLHers, bringing Luca up from the minors to absolutely wreck shop.

And now: Hollander.

It hadn’t made sense at the time. Of course, Zane, like the rest of their team, had been too busy being hyped and astonished when the news hit to think about the reasoning behind it. But when the dust had settled and the offseason made things slow and quiet, somewhere in the neverending cycle of changings and feedings and please, Milo, sleep for more than twenty minutes, baby boy, I’m begging you, he’d realized they didn’t really know why Hollander had decided to come home.

Sure, there had been the press conference, a cheeky half-hour special put on by the Centaurs which said a bunch of things that were actually nothing beyond “Hi, I’m Shane Hollander and I grew up here. You already saw the news so you know where I’ve been. I’ll be playing here now.” There was also, y’know, that night at Monk’s but that had been more about saying hello, not the dirty details.

Zane probably should have realized the dirty details weren’t particularly obscure. Hindsight was twenty-twenty and all that, if ‘all that’ was accidentally opening the door to what he thought was a washroom but was actually a half-assembled trophy room with several very interesting jerseys that did not belong to Boston, Ottawa, or Russia.

The water bottle in his hands flopped to the floor. Thankfully, it stayed closed. He hastily collected it and backed out of the room, closing his eyes tightly as he shut the door. It didn’t matter that he had, it was all superimposed behind his eyes: the cup replicas, the rings, the olympic fucking medals, the trophies he didn’t recognize, and the jerseys, the twenty-four and eighty-one hanging side by side.

“Is everything—oh, shit,” Hollander swore.

Zane flinched away from the door handle like it had burned him. “Wrong door,” he said slowly, forcing eye contact, trying for levity. He could be cool. “Uh, which one’s the washroom?”

All the color had drained from Hollander’s face. “That one,” he said shakily, pointing to the door on the right, just past the laundry room.

Right. It would make sense that they’d share a wall. Plumbing and all that.

And it would make sense that Hollander knew this because his shit was on the wall of Roz’s trophy room, because it wasn’t Roz’s fucking trophy room at all. It was their fucking trophy room. Shane Hollander and Ilya Rozanov’s. Which meant—

Zane sat his ass on the closed toilet seat and put his head in his hands. All the empathy in the goddamn world didn’t do shit if he’d managed to miss his captain—his friend—going through all that.


—


“Shut the fuck up,” Zane told Roz five minutes later, marching across the backyard on a mission, Hollander hot on his heels. “No,” he said over a surprised Russian oath and whatever else he was trying to say to de-escalate. “Just shut the fuck up,” he repeated.

This was probably not the best thing to say. He could practically feel Hollander vibrating with anxiety somewhere behind him, which had probably clued Roz in that something was wrong, but Zane was too busy throwing his arms around Roz and squeezing him within an inch of his fucking life to worry about that at the moment.

“Uh, what is—”

“Trophy room was unlocked,” Hollander sounded sheepish. “He was looking for the washroom.”

“Surprise?” Roz tried joking, but it didn't land. He was rigid in Zane's arms, bracing as if he expected a blow.

Zane hated it. He had just spent five minutes sitting silently on the shitter trying to take stock of how he felt. The breakdown he decided on was thirty percent fury, because Derek Briggs still struggled to look him in the fucking eye after last spring. Luca Haas was cordial, but their chemistry had never completely recovered for Briggs shaped reasons, and seventy percent sadness, because the implication of this, of everything that came with it, was simply beyond any scale Zane could use to measure.

“Fuck,” Zane growled. “I hate that you carried this all alone.”

It was the right thing to say. The honest thing, yes, but more importantly it made Roz sag against him like a puppet with its strings cut. A second later, the sliding door opened and closed. Whether Hollander was trying to give them space or take some for himself, Zane wasn't sure. Roz eventually squeezed him a little too tightly, like he wasn't sure what was too much, then pulled back. Zane made sure to look him in the eye.

“I think,” Roz began, quiet in a way he normally wasn’t, “Maybe we should sit.”

Hollander must have thrown the rags over the back of one of the adirondack chairs when Zane was distracted, the lot of them set in a circle around the massive fire pit they’d prepped for a bonfire, but Roz didn’t bother with those. He gestured back toward the house. The deck was massive, covered, and made for entertaining: a basic grill sat near the kitchen doors near a wet bar and cabinets, the side covered by comfortable looking outdoor furniture: cushioned sectionals and chairs around a fancy looking fire pit. Pyramid heaters flanked the space on stand by. They’d probably be needed for the chilly fall night.

Now that Zane took a good look around, it was pretty obvious that the style here was all Hollander: soothing grays and slates and blue-whites, a pop of color in the cushions and a small army of throw pillows in various outdoor-safe textures. It was more like something you'd see on HGTV. He assumed there was probably a designer responsible.

Roz sat unceremoniously. Zane mirrored him, the fire pit between them. He watched his captain pinch the bridge of his nose and slouch backward, poorly hiding his discomfort.

“So.”

“So,” Roz repeated, blinking.

Zane looked toward the door. “Is, uh, Hollander going to come back?”

“I think so, probably,” He frowned. “He will come back when he is ready.”

Right, Zane thought. “Should we wait, or…”

“Or,” Roz shrugged. “Is me you are upset with. We will talk now.” He exhaled, and flung himself forward, bracing his elbows on his knees. “I know I have been a shitty captain—”

“Whoa, hey, what the fuck?”

“Is true,” Roz sniffed. “I am trying to be better. Shane—having Shane back makes me want to be better for both of us.” He looked around, located the ashtray and pack of cigarettes behind him on a table, and tapped one out of the pack without lighting it. “You know better than most. I am not proud of it, what happened last year with Derek. He and Luca forgave me, but it is more than that. You know this.”

It was Zane's turn to blink, confused. “I don't?”

“You have picked up slack. More than most alternate captains or veterans. I rarely came to events, never really stayed for long, and always fucked off with women when we went out as a team.”

“Not always,” Zane corrected, folding his fingers together on his lap. “And, I mean, you have—had?” He revised on the fly, “A reputation. It wasn't like we didn't know what we were getting into with you.” He shrugged. “You were fine until, y'know, you weren't.”

“Yes,” Roz said heavily. He rolled the unlit cigarette between his fingers, agitated. “And then I, what is,” he snapped his fingers around the cigarette, “Ah, self-destructed. After Tampa.” Another sniff. He sucked at his teeth, cheeks hollowing on one side as he considered what to say. “During, probably. I opened door that was closed. Figured if I was going to die I should be honest, yes?”

Zane nodded attentively. He felt a pang, a hollow echo in chest at that horrible feeling, that fear he was going to leave his wife and unborn son. He'd never forget it for the rest of his life. He'd been so caught up in that feeling that he'd never even clocked Roz beyond a morose presence chain-smoking on their balcony in that hotel room, the guy who'd stumbled in, blind drunk, yet still managed to put a bottle of water on both their bedside tables before passing out on top of his bed.

“You reached out to Hollander,” he realized. “On the plane.”

“Da. Yes.” Roz sighed. “Was very sappy and dramatic, but honest. Had to be good in case it was my only chance to tell the truth.”

“You guys weren't talking?”

“October of twenty sixteen was last time,” he said. “We reconnected after locker cleanout in May.”

The sliding door slid open. Hollander came out, scooted around the fire pit and sat beside Roz. He pulled a lighter from his pocket and handed it over.

“I thought you were hiding lighter on purpose so I would quit,” Roz teased, plucking it from Hollander's fingers, but there was an anxious tic in his jaw.

Rolling his eyes, Hollander said, “Making you smoke outside is enough compromise for now.”

As Zane watched, Hollander shoved in close, forcing Roz to throw an arm around his shoulders or lean away. He chose the first option, exhaling smoke away from Hollander before putting his face in Hollander's hair and breathing.

“I was in trophy room last," he murmured, sounding pained. “Was looking for the glass wipes. Is my fault.”

“It’s okay, Ilya.” Hollander’s eyes were a little bloodshot, but it was the only indication that he might have had his own reaction to Zane accidentally stumbling on their massive fucking secret. “Obviously,” he continued, looking across the fire pit at Zane, “We didn't want you to find out like that, but we figured people would figure it out eventually.” He fanned his fingers across his lower lip. “Probably. It's not, like, a secret or whatever. There’s a picture of us in the living room.”

The picture he was referencing looked like a hockey ad, but okay.

“Back up,” Zane looked between them. Normally he could just follow the flow of a conversation, but Roz wasn't going to get away from dropping a bomb on him, even if it was followed up by him and Hollander being quietly domestic in a way that—well, he thought, that was kind of the thing. “You said you hadn't talked to Hollzy since October of sixteen.”

“Yes.”

Hollander flushed.

Zane gestured to him. “And then you—”

Roz was quick to take the focus off Hollander, Zane noted. “Yes, we did not speak for four and a half years.”

“But you were…”

“Something,” Hollander confirmed.

When Roz looked over at Hollzy, it felt like someone kneed Zane in the gut. It felt like watching someone fall in love right in fucking front of him, that crazy lurch and free fall feeling projected in front of him like a movie. There was this warmth and softness in his gaze that Zane had never seen before, not even with Haas or the handful of rookies he'd consoled over the couple seasons they'd played together. Roz really needed to work on his self esteem, Zane noted. He wasn't perfect, but he was a much better leader than he gave himself credit for.

“We'd been something for a long time,” Roz murmured. “But you know how league was, and Shane—things had happened. You know.”

Zane nodded.

“And so four years later I send desperate—”

“—no, it was sweet, Ilya—”

“—sappy message and I think ‘Shane will never read this, he does not use Instagram but I want him to know if I die that I have only ever loved him—’”

“Goddamn it, Rozanov,” Hollander sniffled, swiping angrily at his eyes like they've betrayed him.

“And I think, for months, that he does not know this. I think he goes public because of fucking Briggs,” Roz huffed, but he was snuffing out his cigarette to wrap both arms around Hollander, crushing him to his chest so Zane doesn't see him cry, “But it wasn't, because Lily is stupid name I put in his phone with my number in rookie season and he had been taking little piece of me onto ice with him since he fucked off to SHL.”

Holy fuck, Zane thought, but what came out of his mouth was a faint, “Shut up.”

“Is true,” Roz said. He jostled Hollander with the hand wrapped around his shoulder. “He did it for me.”

“I did it for us,” Hollander corrected, voice muffled by Roz’s t-shirt. He pulled back, let Roz swipe away the last of the wetness on his cheeks, and returned to his previous position tucked under Roz’s arm.

“That's why you took a deal to come here.”

“I took the deal to win a cup,” Hollander corrected sternly. “Ilya would've done long distance but it would've fucking killed us. Maybe that's selfish, or whatever, but I'm done being miserable to spare bigots from feeling uncomfortable. We just want a future together. So,” he gestured to himself and Ilya, eyes locked uncomfortably with Zane's as if to dare him to challenge his words, “This is us.”

Ilya patted his thigh. “He is on our side, lyubimyy. No need for claws.”

There was, though. Not because Zane was a homophobe—he wasn't, but because Zane was pretty sure Roz needed defending. He deserved to have someone put him first.

Hollander nodded to him, cool and aloof and yeah, Zane thought maybe he thought so, too. The guy was kind of an ice king, really. That composure, despite crying not even two minutes earlier, was downright frosty.

“What can I do to support you?” He asked. “I mean, obviously you're not telling people.”

“We're not not telling people. We bought house together—”

“I'm sorry, what?”

“—Is both our cars in the garage—”

“They're your cars—”

“And you have stolen Mercedes. Even Bood knows it belongs to you.”

“To be fair,” he pointed out, “I just assumed—”

“Not the point,” Roz interrupted. “If they cannot figure it out when I say do not smoke in our house, Shane will be pissed and then they get too drunk to go home and wake up to you making them old man oatmeal—”

“Jesus Christ, it's regular oatmeal—”

“—On the stove in my hoodie and your slutty little running shorts,” he continued over Hollander's protests that Roz’s sweatshirt was appropriately worn and more comfortable and they were normal running shorts—Zane didn't know that he agreed with that last bit, he'd seen Hollzy on the treadmill last week and even Harris had commented that they were low-key scandalous, “Then they are fucking idiots.”

They were looking into each other's eyes, Hollander's chin tilted up a little, Roz’s down, his eyebrows raised expectantly.

“Ilya.”

“Shane.”

They were cute together, Zane decided. He told them so.

Hollander went scarlet. Roz laughed. “You are tomato,” he exclaimed, thrilled. “Moy pomidor.”

Chapter 13

Chapter Notes

The response to last chapter really blew me away. Thank you! ❤️

Somewhere between Boodram sitting on the patio looking like they’d whacked him upside the head with a stick and confirming with Harris that it was okay if he came over early with a photobooth-style setup to take pictures of Shane in his bet-mandated Halloween costume, Shane was pretty sure Ilya had decided he was going to tell the team about them tonight.

Maybe. Probably?

Sometimes it was hard to tell with Ilya. The belligerent tough-guy routine was more likely than not to be a facade, something worn and easy to hide behind when the world asked too much, not his actual personality. The Ilya that Shane knew was sometimes that guy, but deeper down, when it came to his people, he was really fucking considerate.

There may or may not have been have been a two pm freakout about their teammates not having anything they liked to eat, despite the various platters of meat, cheese, and veggies for apps, not to mention several strategically placed bowls of Halloween candy, and the actual meal, which had been carefully reviewed by Ilya with Bood and Shane’s mother (on two separate occasions) to confirm portions were appropriate and dietary restrictions were accommodated. It was kind of cute to see him so obsessive, but also really fucking heartbreaking when Shane got to thinking about it.

This event meant a lot to Ilya, something beyond a housewarming slash halloween slash possible coming out party. This was how he cared about his people. Fiercely. Obsessively. Shane couldn’t help but think of Ilya bent over his counter in Boston, carefully putting together tuna melts with a shoulder high stack of tupperware beside him, making sure everything he put on Shane’s was just right, that the presentation was perfect.

God, he was such an idiot when he was younger. Looking back, it was obvious. It had probably always been.

“I sent Bood out back to build bonfire,” Ilya said when Shane found him in the kitchen, fiddling with the arrangement of the snack trays. Harris would be arriving in an hour and it was probably a little too early to pull everything out, but Shane helped Ilya with a stack of appetizers anyway. “After, he will use downstairs bathroom to shower and put on costume.

“Did you tell him that the towels—”

“Are in the closet next to sink and to put used towel in towel hamper in laundry room? Yes.”

Shane smiled at him. He fucking loved being domestic with Ilya, loved it almost as much as playing hockey on the same team, which was a lot, if he was being honest. He was pretty sure the only things that would top it were playing on the same line, having sex, and winning a cup together.

There was still time for items one and three.

They worked in silence, Ilya removing lids from the round trays and exchanging them with Shane for tongs no one would use but were important to have anyway. They’d had a few renegade tomatoes that jumped off the platters and tried to run away, but it had become a little competition to see who could get to them first and shove them into the other’s mouth. When everything was set up, Shane pulled him toward the stairs.

“We should get a caterer for next time. Someone who can do all this for us,” he told Ilya. “Then you don’t have to worry.”

Ilya frowned. “I am not worried,” he insisted, glowering at Shane as if insulted he would accuse him of such an emotion. “Party will be good.”

“You’re a good host,” Shane said instead of arguing.

“You have done at least half the work,” Ilya gestured to the decorated foyer as they passed through.

“I think that’s how relationships work,” he shrugged. “Come on, let’s get our costumes on before Harris gets here.”

They weren’t particularly fancy costumes, all told. It was more about one of them wearing the other’s jersey, if anything, so the rest of it was all pretty low effort. Considering the surplus of jerseys in their house, Shane had raided their closet instead of asking the pro shop to provide him with another. Ilya gleefully selected one and laid it out on the bed before going back into the walk-in and rifling through the rest of them.

“I am honestly surprised that first time we are doing this is not sex thing.”

Shane traced the letters on the back of Ilya's jersey and huffed, “I don’t know that I’d be into that.”

“I think I could convince you,” Ilya teased, voice sultry. “You would do it for me.”

He wasn’t wrong. Shane probably would.

A moment later, Ilya emerged from their closet a second time, holding up the Centaurs jersey that had been given to Shane when he signed. “Is okay to wear?”

“I thought you were doing the whole sheet-toga thing,” Shane said, but he was nodding. “I heard you telling Dykstra—”

“Meh, I have done this many times. Is boring.” Ilya frowned. “Bad boring,” he clarified.

“Of course,” Shane allowed. “You’re going to go as me, even though I’m the one who lost the bet?”

He shrugged. “Guys will think is funny and Harris will get to take so many pictures. Will be good for social media. Besides: I got eye liner to make freckles, just in case.”

“All I have is a wig.” It wasn’t a very good wig, either. Very Dollarama halloween, but it wasn’t like he’d be wearing it for more than pictures. Just looking at it made Shane’s head itch. “I should have gotten some concealer to cover mine.”

“Wig is enough,” Ilya declared. “Freckles must stay.”

“You’re ridiculous.” Shane felt the smile on his face as he shoved his sweatpants down his legs and kicked them into the hamper in the closet. “So,” he hedged, shimmying into his jeans, “Is this, like, a couples’ costume?”

Ilya pursed his lips as Shane threw a pair of jeans at him. They were about the same color as the ones Shane had just finished buttoning, something Ilya very quickly recognized, looking between Shane and the denim bundle in his arms.

“Maybe, probably, I don’t know.” He paused. “Do you want it to be?”

Shane eyed him. He looked tense. “I just meant that it’s okay if it is, you know?”

“Yes.” Ilya sighed like he was about to admit to something he’d done wrong.

Shane had been ready to go public from the moment he’d sent Ilya that first text in four and a half years. It would have been ugly and stressful and probably not the most comfortable way to go public with a relationship, certainly not the safest way for Ilya, either, but if Ilya had insisted upon it despite the risks, Shane would have done it.

The hardest part had been the separation. The league would probably have its opinions, same with the fans—they hadn’t played in Montreal yet, and Shane was pretty sure it would be an exercise in delayed misery—but there was only one person whose opinion mattered in all this. Shane had controlled the narrative when it came to him and Ilya long enough. Ilya deserved to make this choice for himself.

“I’ll do this however you want,” he said. “Doesn’t have to be perfect or planned.”

“This is what worries me.” Ilya's face fell, fracturing his stoicism. “I do not want you to regret it.”

Shane circled, plucking Ilya’s fingers from the jersey he’d been thoughtlessly smoothing out on the bed. “You could tell people on twitter right now and I wouldn’t regret it,” he began, each word slow and heavy, “I’ll never regret falling in love with you.” He paused long enough to link their hands together at their sides. “Not then, not now, and not ever.” He thunked the knuckles of his free hand against Ilya’s chest, over his heart. “I don’t care how it happens as long as we’re together. Okay?”

“Okay,” Ilya whispered, but he searched Shane’s eyes for a long moment, as if expecting him to add some condition or take it back. When Shane didn’t, he tried for humor. “But if I told Twitter that I am Lily and we are dating you would be very annoyed.”

“I’d delete the app from my phone after retweeting your post,” Shane agreed, “But that’s your call.”

He blinked. “You would really let me do this?”

“I don’t think anyone lets you do anything,” Shane corrected, which couldn’t be any more true.

Ilya needed time to think about it, which was fine. They took turns fixing each other’s costumes: Shane by studying Ilya’s drawn on freckles and adding more on his nose before sidling him with a baseball hat that had to be adjusted for his hair, not to mention his slightly larger head, and Ilya by fussing with Shane’s very sad looking wig.

“I do not think I want to tell whole world today,” Ilya decided, when they were ready to go downstairs again, “But I would like to maybe not be so careful around team. Is not like they’ve noticed anyway and some of them know…”

“Fine, but I draw the line at kissing with tongue in front of our teammates.”

Ilya groaned like Shane had outlawed PDA entirely. “Fine,” he drawled, “No tongue unless we are alone.” He pursed his lips in a familiar invitation. Shane leaned in, shaking his head ruefully at the exaggerated mwah Ilya made as their lips touched. “Ah, caveat for if I am drunk. I will not be able to resist you.”

“Incredible foreshadowing,” Shane remarked, a tiny smile on his face.

“For-what?”

“It’s when you hint that something’s going to happen before it does,” he explained. “How do you know caveat but not foreshadowing?”

“English is stupid,” Ilya deadpanned, reaching for the Hollander jersey on the bed as he mouthed the word to himself to ensure he'd remember it.


—


Later, much later, Ilya was indeed drunk. Not sloppy drunk, but somewhere just past the point of tipsy where he felt good and loose and affectionate. Shane knew this because he found himself with Ilya in his lap, sprawled sideways across an adirondack chair at the cost of his not quite finished can of ginger ale. Shane accepted a wet, smacking kiss that glanced off the corner of his mouth and smelled like an indistinguishable combination of beer and liquor. In front of them, the bonfire that Bood had built and tended for most of the night roared on, a comforting balm against the chilly autumn night.

“Having fun?” Ilya asked. He spoke a little too loudly for how close they were. The flickering firelight bounced off his face, revealing the lack of drawn-on freckles. Shane wasn’t surprised that they hadn’t lasted, Ilya had been running around all night, excitable in a way that had Harris comparing him to his overgrown puppy, Chiron.

Shane nodded. “I am. Are you?”

“I feel fucking amazing,” he grinned against Shane's neck, letting him feel the shape of it.

Earlier, Harris had spent over an hour taking pictures. First he’d posed Shane in front of one of the trees in the back yard with a hockey stick, making him cycle through silly faces (all of which Shane made poorly) from a bunch of popular memes people posted of Ilya to satisfy the conditions of the bet. After, he’d been thrilled to snap a couple of them together, getting several in what he called ‘prom pose.’ Neither Shane nor Ilya had gone to prom, but Harris had insisted. Shane was pretty sure one of them would become Ilya’s phone wallpaper.

Their not-quite impromptu photography session had quickly devolved into madness as the rest of the guys started to trickle in wearing low effort costumes. Thankfully, Harris had brought props while he’d not been planning to work, he’d posted a compilation of their favorites to the team’s socials before logging out for the evening.

The rest of the party had gone well, but was mostly winding down. Some of the guys had already left, going home or moving on to other parties with their significant others if they'd brought them, and a lot of the younger ones had opted to stay inside to take advantage of close proximity to the bar and Ilya’s Switch. Shane could just barely make out the aggressive sounds of Mario Kart being played in the living room, led by Young and LaPointe.

Everyone else was out by the fire.

Beside them, Luca laughed. “You drunk, Cap?”

“I think that's the pot calling the kettle, baby,” Derek cut between their shared chair and Luca’s, offering his boyfriend a bottle of water. He had another in his hand which he indicated with a little shake, but Shane waved it off before Ilya could swat it away like an angry cat.

“I'm not that drunk,” Luca pouted, which made Ilya snicker into Shane’s neck. His slur was significant. “I destroyed you at Mario Kart and beer pong!”

“You won pong because you played with me,” Ilya commented dryly. “You finished fifth in Mario Kart because you got blue-shelled while doing lap three shot with Pointer and gave up when everyone passed you.”

“I would have won if not for the blue shell,” Luca said sadly. “Derek’s bad at video games.”

Derek flinched. “How am I catching strays?”

Amused, Shane murmured in Ilya’s ear, “You're such a bad influence.”

Cheek pressed to Shane’s chest, Ilya looked up at him and replied teasingly, “You love me.”

Eyes still trained on the fire, Shane couldn't help but smile, sinking a hand into Ilya's hair and cradling the back of his head. It made Ilya go even more boneless against him. If he wasn’t drunk, Ilya probably would have told Shane he was being unfair.

“I really do,” he pecked Ilya on the forehead and looked down.

Ilya looked up at him adoringly, which was one of Shane’s favorite expressions even with Ilya’s eyes a little overbright and bloodshot from a combination of alcohol and edibles.

“Uh,” Derek interrupted their moment by murmuring not so eloquently in Shane’s direction, “Are you drunk, too?”

Shane shook his head. “No?” He replied, not sure why he’d made it sound like a question. He’d split a gummy with Ilya—Wyatt had offered when he joined them at the fire, but that was about it.

“Is fine,” Ilya said lazily. “Go back to losing at Mario Kart or Chel or whatever they are playing inside. Take Luca with you before he falls asleep out here. I am too drunk to carry him to guest room. Again.”

Luca had indeed gone a little too hard the last time they'd had him and Derek over, back in Ilya's old place. In his defense, Ilya had goaded him into it, which he was probably thinking about given the look on his face.

“Thanks, dad,” Luca rolled his eyes like a brooding teenager but let Derek peel him out of his seat and lead him in a mostly controlled stumble toward the house.

“Don’t forget to drink your water, malchik,” Ilya called after him. He got a middle finger in response, which betrayed just how wasted Luca actually was. He’d probably be mortified if he remembered in the morning.

With the younger players gone, things quieted down. The fire crackled and spat sparks up into the sky. They looked kind of like little lightning bugs, but it was far too late in the season for that. It still sent a pang of longing through Shane, a desire for warm summer nights at his cottage. Maybe this summer they could invite the team, whoever didn’t have somewhere to go for Canada Day. He wondered if Ilya would like that. Between his place and his parents’, they'd have enough room for everyone, or people could bring tents and camp out by the dock if they wanted…

He looked down, watched Ilya watch the fire, and decided to ask him when they got closer to the end of the season.

It was Harris who broke the silence. “Alright, am I hallucinating or did that,” he gestured at Shane and Ilya, "Just happen?”

Shane took stock. They were definitely getting some strange looks from Dykstra and Chouinard across the fire, but they weren’t hostile, just curious.
