Wyatt, who had plunked himself on the cold, dewy ground despite the excessive number of chairs that had been set out, winked at Shane but continued to roast and distribute marshmallows to the group as if nothing of note was happening.

“I mean,” Troy’s voice was thick with inebriation, which made sense given his proximity to the cooler full of cider he and Harris had contributed, “Yeah, he’s in Shane’s lap, babe, but Roz’s always been a clingy drunk.

“I am not!”

“You definitely are,” Shane teased quietly, poking him in the cheek. “Like an octopus.”

Possibly offended or just pretending to be, Ilya tried to get up, but Shane held firm.

“I didn’t say you had to get up, I was just making a point.”

“Guys,” Harris stressed, arms flailing wildly in their direction. “I'm serious!”

Dykstra made a popping sound with his lips. “Okay, but there’s no way, right? They're fucking with us.”

“Agreed,” Tanner Dillon chimed in around a mouthful of burnt marshmallow, which was still lava hot on the inside. He washed it down with a couple swallows of the nearest drink, which was Troy’s cider.

“Hey!”

Wyatt, who was sitting on the ground beside Shane and Ilya, looked up to Shane and jerked his chin toward the sugary confection in invitation. It immediately caught fire, as if punishing Hazy for looking away.

“Shit.”

“I'll still eat it,” Shane said.

“I thought you didn't eat garbage food,” Troy leaned around Harris, looking longingly at the blackened confection.

“Give it to Troy,” Shane said, rolling his eyes, “I'll have the next one.”

Ilya snorted into his neck as the skewered marshmallow was passed down.

“There's like eighty sticks here,” Wyatt pointed out. They were all in a bucket, which sat next to a little cooler that had everything one needed for s'mores. “You guys are just as close to the fire as I am, you could do it yourselves.”

“Yeah, but Hollzy’s hands are full,” Bood pointed out, winking at Shane, who offered back a quick smile despite the sudden rush of heat to his cheeks that had nothing to do with the fire..

“You make them better,” Dillon added.

A hand flung out from their tangled pile on the chair, Ilya groping around in Wyatt's direction. He managed to find Wyatt's shoulder with his grabby hand gesture. “Give,” he ordered. “I will make.”

“This’ll be good,” Chouinard said.

“Don't do it, Wyatt!”

“Harris,” Ilya sounded annoyed. He had one eye open, which was probably a point for Harris in terms of trusting Ilya to cook anything over an open fire. “I am capable of roasting little ’shmallow.”

Shane hid a smile against the knob of Ilya’s shoulder. Over the summer Shane had told him that he'd called them that as a child, which Ilya had both adored and promptly adopted. In his accent, it came out dramatically Eastern European. Schmah-lohw.

“Dont think I don't see you being a tease over there, Rozanov,” Harris puffed up his chest, gesturing over his shoulder. “You're obviously copying me and Troy.”

“Is my house! I can sit wherever I want!”

“Ragebaiter,” Shane murmured.

Ilya was too pleased to look smug. He wriggled and shifted and somehow managed to land between Shane's feet on the ground. “Give me marshmallow and stick.”

“Hazy, do not—”

“Hazy,” Wyatt referred to himself in the third person, “Knows everything.” He handed over a roasting stick, marshmallow already attached, and looked to Shane. “I'm guessing you want a s'more with the all natural peanut butter cups I found in here?”

Shane's stomach gurgled appreciatively, not that anyone else could hear it. It could also have something to do with the gummy. “Damn right.”

“You have healthy s'mores?” Troy sounded devastated.

“You could have another one, babe,” Harris said easily.

“There are like six bags of marshmallows over here,” Wyatt volunteered. “I don't think the kids ever found them.”

“Derek and Luca know where they are,” Ilya waved toward the house with the hand holding Shane's fireball of a marshmallow.

“Oh my God, put that out before you set someone on fire,” Shane ordered, nudging Ilya's hip with his sneaker and forgetting what he'd wanted to say about Derek and Luca having made s'mores on the patio instead. This bonfire setup was a one time thing, soon to be replaced with an outdoor rink.

“Your ’shmallow,” Ilya declared proudly, holding it up over his head for Shane to blow out in complete disregard for his own safety.

“Menace,” Shane said, willing away the eight different nightmare scenarios offered up by his anxious brain.

Once the marshmallow was put out, Ilya turned his head to the side and pressed a kiss to the inside of Shane's knee.

“Guys, please,” Harris was on his feet now and maybe just a little wobbly. Troy reached for his hips to make sure he didn't tip toward the fire, making Shane kind of wish he maybe hadn’t suggested a bonfire altogether. Maybe after this s’more they should go up on the patio where the little firepit was smaller and less of a hazard. “I'm just saying—”

Ilya was good at knowing when Shane needed that, to the point where sometimes he didn’t even notice he was doing it. His boyfriend was a damn near devastating combination of thoughtful and instinctive.

Somewhere along the way Ilya had been freed from making Shane a s’more, most likely thanks to Wyatt’s intervention (because he did really know everything, Shane was convinced). Shane pressed the leg Ilya wasn't leaning against a little closer, bracketing him in a weird little leg cocoon. A warm hand wrapped around Shane's left ankle, the one closer to Wyatt.

“It's obvious, right? Is what you said.” Ilya set his gaze on the fire.

“I said you were a copy cat,” Harris corrected.

“Leave ‘em alone,” Bood said, tipping the neck of his beer in their direction. “It’s nobody's business.”

Wyatt turned a gleeful look toward Ilya, but said nothing, as if he was going to play stupid until Ilya gave him the okay. Bood, too, who was all in on defending them, even from his teammates, despite the fact that Shane’s situation had accidentally fucked up the team's dynamics last spring. The other guys’ questioning wasn't mean, either. They had just watched Shane and Ilya kiss and cuddle and whatever and rolled with whatever excuse they were given.

And Harris? He just wanted to be excited for them. Shane didn't even need to rack his brain to figure that out.

“Fuck,” Shane blurted, which had nothing to do with the absolutely delectable looking smore Wyatt had just passed to him over Ilya's head. “I am so glad I signed in Ottawa.”

And, well, yeah. That happened. Shane felt himself turning bright red and hoped the fire didn't totally light up his face.

“It's just a s’more, Hollzy.” Wyatt chirped lightly.

Ilya's hand squeezed Shane’s ankle. “You are good friend to cover for us, Hazy,” he said, looking in Wyatt's direction.

“You know too?” Bood looked stunned.

“Kind of obvious, if you keep up with hockey news,” Wyatt offered easily. “You?”

“He wound up in trophy room this morning,” Ilya commented to Wyatt before turning back to Harris. “Yes, Harris, is real, you are only normal cider drunk. Shane and I are together.”

Harris’ sound of excitement was kind of cute, Shane supposed. His drunken teetering not so much but Troy pulled him back down, blinking a little wide-eyed in their direction. Shane was pretty sure he'd just mouthed holy shit, but Dykstra pulled his attention over to the other side of the fire.

“How?”

“When two people like each other very much—” Boodram began, only to be immediately shoved by Chewie.

“Idiot means to say congratulations but also we're confused because you said you had a Montreal girl in playoffs—” he smacked himself in the forehead. “Fucking codenames,” Chewie realized. “Tabarnak.”

“Yes, so smart no one guessed for years and years,” Ilya tipped his head back to look at Shane.

“So why—”

“They don't owe you an explanation,” Wyatt said firmly. It was probably goalie intuition or whatever.

Ilya rolled his head toward Shane's left knee. “Is okay, Hazy.”

“Does Derek know?” Troy had one hand around Harris' waist like a seatbelt, and his usually soft voice was a little gruff.

“He knows.” Ilya swiveled his head back, nose pressed to Shane's jeans. “I maybe accidentally demanded he give Shane's number to me when we were not talking.”

“Accidentally,” Dykstra and Chouinard echoed with air quotes Shane saw out of the corner of his eye.

“Quiet, peanut gallery,” Bood ordered.

“We are good. I was asshole, but he forgave me because he is too nice for his own good, like Shane.”

“You called me an asshole like six times today,” Shane pointed out.

“Potato tomato.”

“That's not the saying and you know it.”

“Yes,” Ilya sniffed, “But I like it better.” Shane's shin received a placating pat. “Anyway,” he continued, as though he was terribly inconvenienced by their banter, “I was terribly jealous and stupid because there is no way Derek could ever handle Shane—”

“Roz, what the fuck?” Derek said. Shane hadn't heard him come back outside. Neither had the others, going by Dykstra’s slack jaw and the squeak that had come from either Troy or Harris..

“He is like baby me, raised by Hollander: no chirps and shittier hands.”

“Ilya!”

“No no, that's fine, shit-talking is how he shows affection,” Derek waved a hand and sat in the perfectly good seat Wyatt had ignored while Ilya gestured at him as if to say See? Someone gets it. Derek continued, “I made sure everyone who left took an Uber, but Luca passed out on the couch. I'll take him home later.”

“You will sleep over,” Ilya insisted, then gestured to Bood, who sat next to the massive drink cooler. “Give him a beer. He is done babysitting for the evening.”

Derek accepted the light beer Bood handed over with a thankful nod. He probably realized that anyone else would've given him an IPA or one of Harris’ family's ciders.

“As I was saying,” Ilya drew everyone's attention back to him, “Derek knows because I am lovesick fool.”

“Actually, Derek—” he sighed as though reliving something unpleasant. Ilya smothered a snort against Shane's leg, “I know because Luca told me. I try to stay out of people's business.”

“Smart man,” Wyatt said, offering his can to clink against Derek's bottle.

“Anyway, now everything is good. We are on same team, we bought beautiful house, Shane is driving my nice car and turning our back yard into ice rink—”

“Hollzy lives here too?”

“Isn't that, like, kind of fast?” Chouinard asked. “No offense?”

“None taken,” Shane told him, “But no, it's not fast at all.” He shrugged helplessly. “I love him.”

“Enough to fight for us to have future,” Ilya added, craning his head back at a comical angle to look at Shane like he was the only other person in the world.

Chapter 14

Chapter Notes

Edit: thanks so much to user kierang for educating me on Canadian candy names (in the US we call them smarties, but in Canada they're rockets).

Sidebar/useless note from someone who grew up a stone's throw from the Peace Bridge: my favorite Canadian candy is coffee crisp. Last time I was in Toronto I nearly bought out a Dollarama's worth of it.

November, 2021
Montreal, QC


The Centaurs didn’t have their first game against Montreal until mid-November, which had almost certainly been done on purpose by the league, either in the hopes that the discourse around Shane's accusations in the spring would die down or maybe to drum up more discourse in the event that there needed to be more eyes on the sport after the initial bump that was Shane’s return in October.

Maybe that was a little conceited or presumptuous, but if it couldn’t be said about this game, one could definitely look at the rest of the series: their next game against Montreal was scheduled just before the trade deadline and the final two—a home and home series—scheduled at the end of March. Anyone with half a mind for the sport could tell it had been done on purpose to orchestrate what the league liked to refer to as ‘storylines.’ Shane had had enough time and distance from the league to accept that he fucking hated that kind of manufactured drama. At least with Ilya and the rivalry there had been an element of competition to drive it.

(It sucked that Shane could already pencil in both March games as ‘scheduled maintenance’ because Coach Wiebe was definitely going to rest him for both, especially if the games went the way the league insiders suggested it was. Sentiment wasn't good, the Metros were worse, and some rough and tumble minor-league-lifer would almost definitely try to end Shane’s career for his five minutes of fame. That was just business.)

Despite the external factors, it was nice to roll into their first game against Montreal knowing that the guys were in on their (not quite a) secret. Shane wasn't stupid enough to think that every guy on the team was progressive and accepting, but the team’s management and core guys were.

It was more than Shane had hoped for despite trying not to expect anything one way or another. He was pretty sure he always had, despite his better judgment. It had happened despite all that scorched earth, grown roots in locker rooms and buses and trains across Sweden, newly out and no longer hiding himself, not that most people there particularly cared about an athlete who wasn’t a footballer, had come up like a weed and refused to die no matter how many times Shane tried to hack at it with more realistic expectations.

Anyway, Shane understood what he was: Asian (the half part both did and didn’t factor into it, depending on who you asked), gay, and in a position that people expected him to use as a platform for social reform. He had to comport himself in a specific way, and had to accept that no matter what he did, he would be held to a certain (different, unfair) standard because of his race and his orientation. Like his mom had told him at the beginning of his professional career, kids like him didn’t see themselves in sport, and even if he just wanted to go out there and play his game, he had to be mindful that the past informed the future. His actions mattered.

So while his teammates answered the Montreal media’s intrusive, non-hockey related questions about Shane’s life choices, Shane himself sat in the visitors lounge and watched the whole thing happen live on the Centaurs’ YouTube channel.

They asked every guy they got in pre-game availability if they’d met ‘the man known as Lily,’ which had sent Ilya into a fit of laughter because, “What are they, fucking stupid? Entire world knows Shane Hollander is gay, obviously secret boyfriend is boyfriend, even if he is named after delicate flower.”

Bood, who was always a media darling—according to Harris, but also Shane’s mom, which was about as glowing a review as one could get—had shrugged and said, “Yeah, I mean, I’ve most of my teammates’ partners. That’s kind of standard when you’re a close knit team like ours.”

Dykstra, who had been the only one Shane, and Ilya, though he’d never admit it, had been worried about, had thrown his head back and laughed. “Dude’s a riot,” he’d said. “Met him a while back.” At their follow up about what Lily was like, he smirked. “Hollzy’s got his hands full.”

They’d been a little harsher on Derek, who had insisted he be made available, which Harris had looked slightly conflicted about, citing what he called ‘the ginger ale incident,’ which had been sweet and a little hilarious because Shane knew Derek didn’t like ginger ale. He preferred Seven Up, which Shane hated because it tasted like rockets, which were a powder-slash-texture thing he’d hated since he was a little kid.

When Derek scrummed, they asked if he’d purposely tried to take heat off Shane last season: “I didn’t,” he'd lied, “But you guys certainly tried to connect the dots, huh,” if he’d known Lily’s identity the entire time: “Shane is a great teammate, the best mentor a guy like me could hope for, and someone I consider family. That means I respect his privacy,” and, possibly more offensively, if he had a problem with it: “Queer people have been involved in sport since long before the MLH was formed, Martin. Not everyone has a public coming out moment, but that doesn’t mean we aren’t here, playing for your favorite teams or rivals.”

“Oh my god,” Luca said, leaning into Ilya to see the coverage on Shane’s ipad. Around them, the visitors lounge had gone silent.

Ilya, as expected really, had been the one to break the silence. “Do you think he knows he just made himself into a headline? He basically came out to fucking Montreal. They are like soap opera journalism,” he snapped his fingers, trying to recall the correct word for what he was trying to describe.

“Tabeloids,” Chouinard called out.

“I could do the same in post game,” Barrett murmured from a lounger in the corner, watching on his phone, though he’d muted it when Ilya complained that the feeds were slightly out of sync. “Harris would probably kill me, but—”

“I think you should do it for yourself,” Ilya said. “Not to protect Derek. He is big boy, yes? Montreal accused him of being with Shane last year, kind of, and he is not like us. He has always been out, just not vocal about it.”

“That’s because he just doesn’t know when to shut his mouth when he does talk,” Luca pointed out. “He means well, though.”

“I know you guys were roommates and played together before,” Chouinard addressed Shane from where he sat at one of the tables near the catering setup with his absolutely disgusting pregame meal, “And obviously media were brutal to him because of,” he gestured with his fork at the Metros logos plastered all over everything in the lounge, “But, I did not know you guys were that close.”

“We are,” Shane said. “He’s a special kid.”

“He’s putting up numbers,” Chouinard pointed out around a mouthful of his usual pasta-based pre-game atrocity that Shane tried very hard not to think about. “He's gonna get that bag next contract.”

“Damn straight,” Luca concurred.

“I think you’re biased, Haasy,” Troy pointed out.

Maybe Haas was, but frankly, the league had a short memory when it came to prospects who dropped out of the draft and came up through other channels years later. At final check, he'd been projected to go in the second round of the 2014 draft, but some scouts had projected him to go eight or ninth, citing a massive ceiling and calling him a sleeper agent. Based on his play, Shane thought he probably would’ve gone in the late twenties if he'd made it to the combine. Unfortunately, his development had been squandered, though not by any fault of his own.

But that wasn’t Shane’s story to tell.


—


Hours later, the Centaurs had won and Shane gave the most boring, bland, and uninformative post game commentary ever recorded. At least, Shane was pretty sure he had. Half of what he was asked was given a very dead-eyed stare, he could feel it in his face, the way it was slack, like he was half asleep, and he’d answered nearly every question (or its follow up) with a firm, “No comment.”

Part of that was an agreement in his settlement not to speak poorly of the franchise he’d successfully held accountable for their actions, even if it was merited. The media still tried in their subtly vindictive way, the same as they always did. They asked questions about his personal life and the settlement and the person he was now, as if he wasn’t the same guy who had played for them for seven seasons, but not about the things that actually mattered.

They didn’t ask about the Montreal fans who came to the game early during warmups in his jersey with their pride flags and signs, how Ilya had pointed out and Derek had shoved Shane toward them with a smile. They didn’t ask about the désolé Hollander chants that rained down when he'd gone to take his first face off of the night and carried on for nearly ten minutes before being contested and later swallowed up by fans calling him a slur when he’d scored against his former team.

It would have accented how the league needed to take a better stance to defend its players against hatred it had allowed to fester and take root, would have been something Shane wouldn't have been able to speak on anyway, but it was still the principle of the thing. Instead, Montreal’s journalists had been too busy asking in indirect, obtuse ways if Shane felt remorse about the Metros being at the bottom of the division and losing their first round pick that could have been the first overall they so desperately needed to rebuild as a result of the accusations that had been brought against the organization.

Ilya had spoken after him, and his scrum hadn’t gone much better. His usual swagger had melted away in favor of something sharper and defensive.

Shane hadn’t heard what he’d said, but he knew that Harris had intervened, cutting Ilya off mid-sentence and indicating that the press was no longer welcome in the visitor’s locker room.

“I don’t want to speak to them again unless we play them in the playoffs,” Shane said when the last reporter had been sent down the hall and Harris had finally taken his hands off his hips in the doorway to consult his ever-present clipboard, which was held together by stickers and pride tape in a rainbow themed explosion.

“I can’t promise anything, but I’ll do my best to keep them away from you,” Harris agreed before turning his attention to Ilya, who hadn’t even made a comment about how there was no way in hell that they’d drag themselves into a wildcard spot based on how shitty they’d become. “You okay, Roz?”

“Angry,” Ilya responded, throwing his bundled up, sweat soaked socks into the laundry bin in the middle of the room with a little too much force behind it. “I will shower,” he decided, stripping down and stalking off in the direction of the showers like a frustrated predator.

“What’d they say to him?” Shane asked Harris, who flinched.

“Mostly the same as they asked the guys earlier, but,” he shrugged. “I think it was just a little much.”

“They were saying shit all game,” Bood agreed, coming out of the showers in a towel. Luca wasn’t far behind him, pale hair plastered to his head like he’d all but ran out of there once Ilya entered. It was probably for the best to give him some space.

“We knew to expect it though,” Shane pointed out.

It hadn’t been as bad as he had been expecting, if he was being honest with himself. The attention on him was normal, but the guys had done a really good job of deflecting, answering the media’s questions without giving them anything. The Metros who remembered playing with him had been a mixed bag: some blatantly ignored him, others snuck looks when they thought he wasn’t paying attention, and a few of the ones who had always had things to say had been vocal. The Metros organization was old school. Just because they’d had a change at the top didn’t mean the effects had trickled down quite yet. There wasn't any headhunting, which Shane had been anticipating.

“Yeah, but you’re… you,” Harris pointed out, mindful of the other staff milling about the room.

When Ilya emerged ten minutes later, skin pink from too-hot water and scowl smoothing his face into something distant and aloof, Shane patted the bench next to his stall and waited for Ilya to throw himself down in a chaotic, towel-clad heap. He pressed his face into Shane’s neck, wet curls dripping onto Shane’s suit, and breathed, raggedly.

“You deserved so much better,” Ilya murmured, letting his head rest on Shane’s shoulder. He sounded heartbroken.

Shane wasn’t sad. Maybe he should have been; Maybe that was why Ilya was sad for him. It hurt Shane more that Ilya was distraught than it ever would to hear the Montreal crowd call him a faggot.

“I have it,” he told Ilya, looking around the room, at the guys who had their backs. “Here. With you, and our team.”

“Fuck you, Hollzy,” Dykstra yelled across the room, sniffling.

The rest of the guys seemed to echo the sentiment.

Chapter 15

Chapter Notes

My toxic trait is putting a lot of old trauma into my works. It's not all one for one but writing this series has had me pulling from a lot of tender places. Honestly, that's a good thing, even if this chapter fought me on it the whole way.

They weren’t about to set any records, but the Centaurs were second in the Atlantic division and third in the conference at the end of the month. Shane had even gotten to spend a night with Hayden, Jackie, and their kids in Boston around American Thanksgiving, which had been emotional in a good way, even though Ilya had told anyone who would listen that Shane was ‘fraternizing with the enemy,’ like he wasn’t just as excited to get time with Marley and Svetlana, who had also been invited to the Pike’s first American turkey-day celebration, as the kids called it.

The schedule this time of year always felt compressed, games smashed close together between the American holiday at the end of November which the league half-celebrated, half-ignored due to football, and the league’s mandatory travel restrictions around Christmas which made everything more difficult despite the player’s association fighting for the day off.

So naturally, the Centaurs ended the month of November with a western roadtrip.

Admittedly, of all the games Shane would play in what Ilya and some of the guys joked around as being Shane’s welcome back tour—partially because several teams, like Boston, New York, and Washington, had finished games with the kind of handshake line saved for the end of a playoff series or a guy’s final game in the league, their captains lingering awkwardly at center ice trying to catch Shane’s eye, wave him over for the obligatory hand clasps and shoulder pats—the one Shane was actually kind of nervous for was Colorado.

Not because of their team, which was good—the Centaurs would have their work cut out for them with Colorado's defense—but because it meant he couldn't avoid the inevitable.

Colorado’s social media manager, a very nice woman named Steph, had FaceTimed Harris the day before to ask about doing a reunion video in which JJ would be waiting for Shane when the Centaurs’ bus arrived at the Denver arena for pre-game skate. She’d pitched it as being a very natural thing, no sound bites required. When Shane declined, she'd offered not to have audio at all.

That hadn't changed Shane's answer. He understood that JJ was a beloved veteran in Colorado’s room, that it would even be good press for them both. JJ had more cups now than Shane now, having been with Shane for two, then off to Colorado to win one on his own. As he'd tried to explain to Harris, he didn’t owe anybody that kind of access to his relationships.

Things with JJ were complicated. They hadn’t really parted on good terms—or at least, Shane had parted with him the same way he had with everyone else: complete withdrawal without so much as a word. JJ hadn't taken it well. Like Hayden, he’d lit up Shane on Instagram. The difference lay in his messages, which had started with sad, disbelieving rants and quickly became aggressive, rage-filled missives that eventually petered out into something worse: a massive guilt trip.

JJ had touched upon a wound with his words, made it seem like Shane owed him something: an explanation, a reason, as if Shane could only pull away if he justified it first. He had no idea what Shane had gone through back then, but now that he did, because Shane’s life had been made public in the pursuit of something greater, JJ and his team wanted the whole world to see… what, exactly? What kind of reunion would it be if Shane went through with it? He wasn't even sure he wanted to speak to JJ, if he was being honest.

Shane knew he hadn't been the greatest friend and that the messages were years old, that he probably should let them go, but it was different when it was someone he'd cared about accusing him of not caring enough for the people around him. Maybe he hadn't done things the right way, but wasn't like he'd wanted to isolate himself. All he'd been trying to do was keep JJ and Hayden, his closest friends, from losing their careers by association.

But of course, there JJ was, waiting in the parking lot when the bus pulled up outside Ball Arena.

It pissed Shane off more than the media circus that was Montreal. Ilya, who had been chatting with Haas across the aisle, closed his hand over Shane's. It wasn't enough to soothe him.

Of course, when he realized why Shane was angry, spotting Dejenais leaning against the building in his suit, a cigarette hanging from his mouth, he met Shane on his level. It probably wasn't a good thing.

“Do you want me to handle it?” Ilya whispered.

Shane shook his head. “Absolutely not.”

“I am your captain,” Ilya reasoned. “Would not be abnormal—”

“I'm pretty sure he’s figured us out.”

“Did Hayden say?”

“Nope,” the word felt full of bees, buzzy and wrong in Shane's mouth. “He's not talking to Hayden right now.” There were very few reasons why JJ would simultaneously pull away from Hayden’s attempts at mediation and press Shane through external, unavoidable methods of communication, he thought, but couldn't bring himself to say.

“Fuck,” Ilya murmured, thunking his head against Shane's shoulder, probably figuring it out for himself.

“I have to deal with it, I guess,” Shane said, hating the feeling of pressure and confrontation. It reminded him of Montreal, not that first conversation with management, where he'd expected the best, but the last ones, the ones where he'd known he was marching to an execution, expected to validate someone else's unrealistic expectations because to them he was a commodity, not a person.

And once again, he'd have to fucking do it, or else some security guard or random media person with a press badge and a cell phone would spin him as the asshole.

“Someone could distract him so you could get away,” Ilya tried. He always tried so hard to fix Shane's problems for him.

“I don't want to have it out on the ice on fucking national TV. That'd make it worse.” Shane blew out a sigh. “At least their social team listened to my requests and there aren't any cameras.” At least not any that Shane could see, anyway.

“Okay,” Ilya said, lips pressed to the knob of his shoulder. He didn't sound like it was. “You will call if you need me.”

Shane nodded.

What followed was Ilya wrangling the team off the bus like there was a prize waiting for them in the visitor's locker room. There wasn't. Colorado's in-arena restaurant was the same as all the other arenas around the league when it came to pre-game catering, so pasta and dried out chicken breasts it was, assuming they didn't take their chances with the hotel restaurant.

He took up the rear, jerking his head to indicate that the guys should indeed follow Ilya and not linger with him. Derek was the only one who protested, pressing a hand to the small of Luca's back to ensure he moved on.

“Go with them.”

“I see what's happening here,” Derek said. Together he and Shane stepped off the asphalt and up onto the curb. “I don't think you should be—”

Shane turned his head to the side and hoped his bland look said everything Derek needed to know.

“Shane? Shane!”

It was too late.

JJ had decided enough of the team had gone into the building. He pushed himself off the exterior brick wall, snuffing out his cigarette and flicking it into a strand of Christmas lights illuminating the landscaping, already making his approach.

“It's okay,” Shane told Derek. It wasn't, and Derek was looking at him like he knew it wasn't, but there was nothing else to be done.

“I know what that asshole said to you,” he said loud enough for JJ to hear.

Softer, Shane replied, “I know.”

Derek had, for better or worse, had a front row seat to a lot of Shane’s business, though the incident in question had been an accident. There had been a phone mixup early in their Swedish cohabitation that had resulted in some very nasty messages from JJ finding their way in front of Derek’s eyeballs.

“It’s none of your business, little man,” JJ called out, nearly upon them. “It was years ago.”

“What a fucking joke,” Derek spat at the ground between them.

Up ahead, Chouinard looked back at them from the doors to the player entrance, concerned. Shane shook his head. He took a second to make sure nobody else was watching, then jerked his head toward Chewie and the closing doors between them in a silent gesture to please just leave it. The sooner everyone left, the sooner this would be over with.

Sensing that Derek wasn't going to listen—his temper wasn’t easy to spark and even harder to stamp out—Shane put a hand on his shoulder and pushed gently in the direction of the door. “Go on, Derek. I’ll catch up in a minute.”

“Yes, listen to your maman,” instigated JJ, because he'd always been good at picking at people.

“You don’t get to talk to him like that,” Shane said as Derek bristled, shoulders rising toward his ears but obeyed.

“Why not? Sources say you two are like family.”

The words felt wrong. They sounded slimy, like Shane had done something wrong, but that didn’t make sense. There was nothing wrong with him being close to Derek, and that closeness had absolutely nothing to do with the league they played in now. If anything, the opposite was true.

Changing the subject was easier than trying to figure out what JJ wasn’t saying, though, so Shane started over. “I thought I said no to this,” he said. He felt his voice waver and tried to figure out if it was anxiety or some other emotion. He wasn’t sure.

JJ shrugged. “You said no to the video.” He crossed his arms over his chest. “Not to talking.”

Shane waited until he couldn't see his teammates through the doors, then started in that direction. He knew JJ would follow him. “Did you consider that maybe I said no to the video because I didn't want to talk?”

“Too busy with Boston Lily to make time for your friends?”

Reeling, Shane snapped, “What the—is that what this is about?”

Whirling around, Shane willed himself not to shove JJ to the brick just outside the entrance and tell him to shut his face. The little tunnel-like overhang near the player entrance cast shadows that made it seem private, but sound carried, and Shane knew anyone who pulled into the lot could see them.

JJ continued, purposely trying to goad him. “Hayden was convenient, but I must have been too far out of your way.”

“We're done here,” Shane forced himself to say. He could feel his chest heaving. He hated confrontation, needed to disengage. He’d gotten the door open when JJ grabbed his wrist.

“No we're not.” JJ held firm, the two of them suspended, half inside, half out. “Tabarnak,” He cursed. “Hayden might think you can do no wrong, but I thought we were friends. Friends do not fuck off without a word, for no fucking reason. Friends stand up for each other. You didn’t even give us the chance.”

The words were, in a way, both better and worse than what Shane had been expecting. Better, because they weren’t terribly unfamiliar. Worse, because they hadn’t changed, despite everything.

“I don't owe you an explanation,” Shane told him, managing to use the place where they were joined to tug them both fully inside. Their eyes met and it hurt. It felt like spikes, making it almost physically painful to hold JJ’s furious gaze. “I’m sorry if that upsets you, but you have no idea what I've been through.”

“That is not true,” JJ insisted. His voice echoed sharply down the hall. “Everyone knows what you have been through because you gave them a front row seat. Told the whole world, but not your friends when it counted. We had years together and I had to find out like a fucking idiot that Lily—”

Ice flooded Shane’s veins. It was like he blinked and his hand was clamped over JJ’s mouth like a vice. He didn't remember thinking about it. “No,” he said flatly. He removed his hand when JJ stilled. “You don’t get to do that.”

“I don't? You kept that from the whole world, too, even if they do not know about it.” JJ didn't seem to care about what Shane wanted, but rather his own hurts. “Are you afraid he won’t come out and defend you?”

“I don’t need him to defend me.” Shane looked him up and down and decided that JJ, for how shitty he was behaving, probably wasn’t shitty enough to out Ilya. At least, he hoped. “Was this how you were going to have this conversation with cameras on us?”

“At least if the cameras were on you, you might have talked,” JJ admitted. He shoved his hands in his pockets, his disappointment a weight in his tone. “I just wanted to know why.”

“I’m not allowed to talk about it,” he told JJ. The cresting wave of emotion, the horror-shock-anger of confrontation had yielded, leaving the frenetic energy that was nerves or anxiety or whatever the fuck it was Shane was feeling with nowhere to go. “Even if I wanted to tell you, which I don’t.”

JJ nodded, jaw working. “So that is it? Goodbye to years of friendship?”

He could feel his lips trembling, hated the feeling that he was disappointing someone, even if whatever friendship they’d had felt so far removed from where they were now. “I guess,” he said. “You’re the one who gave it conditions.”

Walking away felt about as terrible as JJ scoffing at his back, but it had to be done. Pretending it was fine would have been worse.

It was funny, Shane thought, sliding into the dressing room on autopilot and lowering himself to the bench in front of his stall, letting his head drop into the curved bowl of his hands. People always talked about sticking up for yourself as being this thing that always felt so good and empowering, but they rarely talked about the feelings of inadequacy or disappointment that came from establishing boundaries and learning that the people you cared about didn’t care for you in the same way, that their love and friendship had conditions, that maybe they weren’t the people you thought they were, and no amount of time or distance would ever really change it.


—


It was no surprise Shane played terribly in their game that evening. He’d spent the afternoon before the game half-present, Derek and Ilya losing their collective shit in a way that had Luca sitting beside Shane, looking concerned by the weird protectiveness that had taken root in them.

His focus was fucked, his mind simultaneously stuck, half frozen, and bouncing off the walls of his skull like a pinball, unstoppable motion. He had no idea how he played—or, that wasn't right—he knew how he played, the memory of it just didn't stick. It was the wrong side of instinct. The survival side of it, not the competitive edge, had been what propelled him through the game and its aftermath.

Sometime later, he came back to himself—not that he’d left, but his mind had gone quiet and distant for a while the way it did when things got to be too much—in his and Ilya’s hotel room, Derek sitting on the bed beside him, an ice pack on his hand, Ilya pacing by the window, Wyatt and Luca playing cards at the table like this was a normal hangout on any other roadie. A part of him was distantly annoyed by it all. He didn't want to be seen like this or have to deal with other people, but another, quieter part of him realized it didn't have anything to do with him at all. They—Luca and Hayes—were here for Ilya and Derek, because Shane wasn't the only person here who needed someone in his corner tonight.

The game hadn’t been especially brutal, but it had been charged with emotion. Those emotions had mostly bounced off Shane, but that didn’t mean others needed time for them to run their course.

“I don't want to fucking hear it, Hayden!” Ilya was raving, the hand not holding his phone to his ear slashing aggressively through the air in front of him. “Saying sorry is empty words. He wasn't sorry enough to show Shane even a little fucking respect and now he feels bad. Is not Shane's problem he is shitty person.”

He fell silent as Hayden responded, words a blur of familiar tone and cadence from Shane's spot on the bed. Ilya's sharp motions ceased so he could focus on what was being said, though when he turned around and noticed Shane looking at him, he approached.

Instead of touching, which Shane wasn't sure he wanted right now, everything feeling a bit too intense, Ilya pulled the ice off Derek's hand. “One second, Hayden,” he interrupted. “Off for twenty,” he instructed Derek, handing him the melted bag of ice. Over his shoulder he added, "Haasy, where is your timer? I have been on phone for half an hour.”

Luca set his hand face down on the table, reaching for his phone. “Shit, I thought I set it. Sorry, Derek.”

“It’s okay,” Derek said. “I’m going to refill the ice bucket.”

Shane felt the mattress shift when Derek rose, his body bobbing with the motion like a buoy in a lake, tethered but unsteady. Seconds later, it dipped again, but with a heavy sigh and warm skin that pressed firmly against Shane’s arm.

He could smell Ilya’s cologne layered over the scent of the usual not quite unscented three-in-one that was in every locker room in every arena, and turned his head toward it. If they were alone, he’d have shoved his head under Ilya’s chin.

“I am going now,” Ilya said into the phone. “I will text you. Tell that fucker—” Ilya looked toward the ceiling and said something in Russian, but Hayden was already interrupting. He listened for a moment and then, “Yes. Thank you, Pike.” He paused, fingers finding Shane’s knee and squeezing gently. “Goodbye.”

“Should we—”

Ilya tossed his phone up toward the headboard. “We should eat something,” he looked to Wyatt. “Room service?”

“Hotel restaurant closed an hour ago.”

“Boo,” Ilya jeered, jostling Shane, just a little. “Is anywhere within walking distance?”

“You want to walk somewhere?” Haas asked. “It’s fucking freezing out.”

“We play sport on ice.”

“It’s climate controlled,” Luca protested.

Shane snorted softly. It was loud to him, but probably more like a slightly exaggerated exhale.

Ilya’s lips brushed his temple. “He is being baby, ah?”

The lopsided shrug Shane offered in response made Ilya rock against him shoulder to shoulder.

A clicking sound followed by the whir of a small motor signalled Derek’s return from the hall. He stepped in and offered Ilya his keycard back, then dropped the refilled ice bucket on the table by Haas and Hayes’ forgotten card game.

Another kiss landed in Shane’s hair. It didn’t feel heavy or uncomfortable, but to be fair, Shane was pretty sure Ilya’s presence never had. They watched Luca and Derek wriggle around, the pair of them too big for the rickety chair Luca had previously been occupying alone.

Lips found the shell of his ear next. “Hungry?”

Shane hadn’t eaten anything since long before the game, which was not ideal, but his stomach had been as unsettled as the rest of him since arriving to the arena. This sort of thing used to happen a lot more often. Today was an uncomfortable exception, which Shane probably should have expected and warned Ilya about.

He had a sneaking suspicion that someone who also knew what Shane was like when he was overwhelmed—several someones, actually, considering Ilya’s call to Hayden—had provided him with coaching, or maybe just information.

Or maybe Ilya just knew him really fucking well.

“I could eat,” Shane decided, knowing that the yawning void in his stomach didn’t bother him now, but it probably would when he tried to sleep. He didn’t feel nauseous or anything, so that was as good a sign as any. “Are you okay?”

Ilya’s lips found his shoulder again. “I am angry,” he admitted, “At situation. Hayden is handling it. Derek did not break anything when he punched that asshole,” he very pointedly did not say JJ’s name, Shane noticed, “which is only reason I am not angry at him, too.”

“His face looked punchable,” Derek protested, “You said it yourself.”

“Yes, but I was going to do it!”

“Nobody should have done it,” Shane said. He shivered, still feeling a little weird and out of place.

Ilya seemed to consider something. He tugged his hoodie over his head and dropped it into Shane’s lap.

It smelled good and was warm. Shane wriggled into it. “Derek give you tips?”

“You wear Derek’s hoodies?” Ilya’s attention focused on Shane in a way that felt sharp.

From the other side of the room, Derek corrected, groaning, “Wore, Roz, please. It’s not like that. It happened maybe twice?”

“I will give you every hoodie I own,” Ilya decided.

“Are we eating or are we eating?” Hayes spoke over him, instantly de-escalating the situation. “There’s a Wild Wings and a McDonalds.”

“The McRib is back,” Luca commented. “Young and Bergy were talking about it.”

Shane could practically hear Ilya licking his chops. Neither place sounded particularly great, and he would probably bitch about how gross he felt tomorrow—

“That is not healthy enough,” Ilya argued on his behalf.

“Like wings are any better?” Wyatt asked.

—but, if he was being honest with himself (and he was trying very hard to do that instead of shoving all of his thoughts and feelings down and letting the world tell him how he ought to live his life), “Last time Ilya ordered McDonalds, he ate all the chicken mcnuggets before I got one.”

Ilya jumped up, hand pressed dramatically to his chest. “You said you didn’t want any!” He exclaimed, before promptly doing a one-eighty, his expression smoothing into something serious. “Okay, boys, go get your jackets. Meet in the lobby in ten.”


—


“I'm sorry.”

Ilya rolled his eyes. When Shane’s expression remained serious, he took another step, coming fully out of the steamy bathroom. “You are sorry for what? Stinking up bathroom? McDonald's shit has nothing on that awful fucking protein powder you tried over the summer.”

While Shane was generally adverse to toilet humor, pun intended in this case, it probably would've made him crack. Maybe. Hard to tell. People didn't talk about what living together is like in that regard: that your partner is also human and disgusting bodily functions will be happening in close proximity to your person, which also has disgusting bodily functions because, again, human.

“I was apologizing for last night,” Shane explained, gently shaking his head. “I saw JJ’s post game.”

Ilya made a strangled sound, as if his anger and concern were fighting each other. “Why would you watch that? You were despot—despondent?” Shane nodded, “Despondent,” Ilya stressed, “Most of yesterday. Do not worry about him.”

“He knows about us.” To be clear, Shane had told Ilya this yesterday afternoon, long before JJ told the media, I hope he and Boston Lily are happy together, in a begrudging post game statement that had instantly garnered the entire hockey world’s attention. He was sporting a black eye and a cut on his cheekbone from a combination of his visor and Derek's knuckles.

“And? He is small, pathetic man who does not deserve your friendship. Pike is furious, says he will murder him. They play in Boston in three weeks.” He grinned. “Marley says he is looking forward to it.”

“Great. Because Hayden fighting JJ will definitely convince people to let it go,” Shane grumbled. His head wound up in his hands.

“Ah, no no no, none of that.” Ilya dropped his towel on the floor and plunked his knees on top of it—hotel floors were disgusting and Shane had ranted about it more than once—to worm his way into Shane's line of sight. “Boston Lily could be a lot of guys. Marley and Hayden will distract press. Even Yuna thought Marley was Lily and she is smartest person we know.”

“Amazing,” Shane groaned, shaking his head.

Ilya's eyes softened. “Hey,” he said gently, squeezing Shane's hands, “Shane. It’s okay if JJ’s stupid comment is what outs me. Is not like it is really a secret.”

It was true. Shane knew it was. “I want you to be able to make that decision for yourself.”

Shrugging, Ilya brought their hands together and kissed the top of Shane's palms. “You want for me to have what you did not. Is kind, but unnecessary. All I want is you. I do not care who knows this or how they find out as long as it is still you and me together.”

Shane's smile was small. Ilya matched it.

“There will never be a perfect time,” he continued. “Is why I agreed to the world finding out when they find out, why you did not make crazy plan. It doesn't work that way.” He shook their combined hands for emphasis. “This belongs to us.”

Shane added that to the bank of mantras in his head, the ones he needed and the ones he didn't. We don't owe them anything and This belongs to us weren't quite the same thing. One was more possessive, more protective, like cupping your hands around a candle to protect it from blowing out in the wind.

It wasn't like Shane didn't know these things, but it was difficult to rise above the noise sometimes, especially when people were pushing in on all sides, Shane being a public figure first, and not a person to the vast majority of those who knew of him.

He sighed, let his head thunk forward, gently headbutting Ilya so they rested forehead to forehead. He was folded over in an awkward position, but it didn't matter.

“You're right,” he murmured. “I think I needed to hear that.”

Ilya's hands came up to stroke his cheeks. “I am very wise,” his voice was warm, like his fingers. “Is about time you noticed.”

Shane huffed, lifting his chin and sneaking a kiss to Ilya's forehead. “Did you save me any hot water, wise one?”

“Mm, no,” he teased. “Hotel is out. Only cold showers.”

Chapter 16

December, 2021
Ottawa, ON


If Ilya was surprised to find Derek on one of the couches in their living room, wrapped in the soft blanket he’d had someone ship over from his place in Sweden and drinking the lavender mint tea Shane preferred to coffee when he was anxious and couldn’t stomach caffeine while watching ESPN analysts talk about football, he didn't say anything.

They, meaning Shane and Derek, had been in and out of optional skate early, off the ice before the bulk of the team showed up and in the weight room while Ilya and Bood teamed up to help the coaches with drills. Luca had been fine, laughing and joking with the younger players like usual when Shane saw him in passing. None of them had noticed that Derek was being distant, which meant he’d been using Shane as cover.

That had been enough to tip Shane off to the fact that Derek was not sharing necessary information with his partner, but he’d been polite enough not to call him out on it at the practice facility. Unfortunately for Derek, Shane’s politeness translated to a silent, added pressure that forced him to crack just as soon as practice was over, hence his presence on the couch, wrapped in the blanket he’d tried to steal from Shane when he’d left for Canada the summer before last.

Ilya didn't push, going to the fridge to pull out a premade recovery shake—whoever got home from practice made two. Judging by the rattling around he was also dumping one of the bottles of nesquik he kept in the door into it because he didn’t trust Shane to make them sweet enough.

It was an affront to Shane’s ethically sourced cocoa powder, but relationships were built on compromise.

“Is too early to be that day, yes?” Ilya called from the kitchen. He emerged frowning at his phone, swiping loudly at it one handed a welcome reprieve from the analysts onscreen discussing a sport Shane positively loathed. “We played Admirals in New York like… two days before Barrett was traded from Toronto and freed from homophobic prison.”

Derek offered a shuddery exhale. “You are way more perceptive than people give you credit for,” he murmured.

“No, no, I am mean captain,” Ilya rather dramatically pressed the hand with his phone in it to his heart. “And you only feel comfortable saying this because Shane is here to protect you,” he needled. He placed his sweating shaker bottle on the coffee table directly next to the little coaster rack.

“You’re going to need protection from me if you don’t use a coaster, asshole,” Shane retorted, glaring at his drink and the tiny halo of condensation pooling around it. Ilya waggled his eyebrows because he loved getting bitched at, even though he complied. That handled, Shane saved Derek from having to explain himself. “Front Office sent an email about the fathers trip last night.”

Ilya pulled a face. “Around Christmastime again? We should be at hospital doing fun holiday activities with sick children. I will complain to Harris to get it rescheduled.” His eyebrows dipped, drawing closer together as he stabbed at his phone some more.

“It’s a little late for that,” Shane told him, patting the seat beside him so Ilya would stop looming between the couches like a weird referee. “Trip announcement’s already on social media. Marketing made a reel this morning and blindsided him by asking who he was bringing,” Shane finished, tipping his head in Derek’s direction.

“And I clammed up,” Derek winced.

The skin around Ilya’s eyes tightened as he squinted in confusion. “Clammed? What?”

Shane mimicked a clamshell folding inward with his hands, the base of his palms acting as a hinge.

“Ah. Another stupid English thing.” Ilya threw himself at the far side of the couch, just out of Shane’s reach. “Not that you are unwelcome here,” he broached carefully, “But why are you not talking to Luca about this?”

Derek wilted. “He’s excited to have his dad come over from Switzerland. He’s already talking to his family about them coming to Ottawa to meet them after the trip and stay for the holidays.”

Confused, Ilya looked to Shane, and when Shane’s face didn’t give him anything to go on, he focused on Derek again. “Is that not a good thing? Is family. Will be nice time.”

“I don’t,” a sigh. “I'm really bad at family things,” Derek told him. “It's like, I don't know how to fit in and I just feel like an outsider. It's unpleasant.” He winced again. “I mean, I want to meet Luca’s family and I'm sure they're nice—”

“Nobody’s doubting that,” Shane cut in, unwilling to let Derek spiral about it

He wouldn’t pretend he understood in practice, but he kind of got what Derek was saying. His parents hadn’t come for those kinds of events when they played in Sweden, and outside of Rose joining once for a mentor's weekend (never again), they’d hung out together while the other guys had their parents or siblings to entertain. It was awkward to have everyone around you be with someone they were related to or close with while you were alone. It amplified the feeling, even for someone like Shane, who had a very supportive immediate family.

“Is not fun for me, either,” Ilya admitted, a little tentative. “Bood and I have arrangement.” He kept his words light as he continued, “I make rounds to keep up appearances, but,” Ilya's smile didn't meet his eyes, “Bood is in charge of family things.” As if remembering it wasn’t just him anymore, he looked to Shane. “Is David—”

Shane shook his head. “No. They’re going on that cruise over the holidays, remember?” He rubbed his chin as he thought about it. Last night he'd marked himself as not bringing anyone without much thought and considered it done. “But, um, even if they weren’t, I think it would be really awkward, given everything, and I don’t want to put them in an uncomfortable position.”

That seemed to relax Ilya a little, enough to joke, “It would be funny to see Yuna tell the league to fuck off on the mother’s trip.”

“It would, and then I’d be fined within an inch of my life and probably get kicked out for someone in my camp violating the non-disparagement clause of my very public settlement.”

Ilya made a psh sound with his lips.

Shane rolled his eyes. “You’re such a child.”

Derek huffed quietly into his blanket.

They received a raspberry in response.

“Can we please get back to the subject at hand?” Shane pleaded, though he made a mental note to have his mom tell Ilya about how bitchy she’d found the other Metros’ moms, leading to her ‘accidental’ blocking of the moms’ group chat following a disastrous mini golfing excursion in the spring of 2013.

“Don’t rush on my account,” Derek slurped at his tea. When they didn’t continue rubbing each other, he groaned. “I’d like to be distracted instead of dealing with our upcoming road trip weekend and its extremely poor timing, followed by several weeks of living with my boyfriend’s immediate family celebrating holidays that are very painful for me.”

“You do not have to do this,” Ilya pointed out. “You could talk to Luca.”

Derek rolled his eyes. “And say what? Luca’s already excited about it. He wants all his favorite people together.”

“But you should still tell Luca that it is hard for you and that you will need space,” Ilya pointed out. “His family can stay at hotel.”

Ilya picked at his cuticles, which was something Shane hadn’t seen him do before.

“Is difficult with families who are good, sometimes,” he continued. “Yuna and David were very gentle with me about last few years, because we have all gone through same thing, but Shane asked them not to talk to me about family things—”

Wow, Shane thought, unable to help the wave of affection he felt. Not just as Ilya’s boyfriend, but as a teammate, as family in the sense of their sport, the captain of their team. He was such an incredible person, peeling back the rough armor, and exposing his fragile insides to help someone who was experiencing something not so different, even though it had to hurt to dredge it up from behind the walls he’d built to keep it from tearing him apart. Shane didn’t know how he did it.

Ilya went on. “But it does not stop them from asking. Probably they are curious, but is also how they show care. They do not understand, and that is good for them. They are lucky not to,” he added quietly, “But this does not make it suck any less.”

Swallowing, Shane kicked a leg out to nudge against Ilya’s thigh since he was out of arm’s reach. Ilya’s hand closed over his ankle bone, thumb swiping over the ridges of his crew socks. A little selfishly, he hoped that all this talking would help Derek without making Ilya feel worse. That line of thinking didn't stop Shane from adding, “My mom brought up everything I asked her not to by the fifth time we had dinner with them?” Ilya nodded once in Shane’s periphery. It had been late in the summer. “We fought about it. You’ve met my mom, so you can imagine how well she took that.”

The hand around his ankle stilled as Derek winced. Both were probably the correct response.

To say Yuna hadn’t taken it well was an understatement, but Shane had been very clear when he said Do not bring up Ilya’s brother or how he feels about Ilya’s life choices. Family in general is a difficult subject, so let’s just avoid it, okay?

In Yuna Hollander fashion, not only had she broached the subject of Alexei, but she’d also asked about Ilya’s mom. Ilya had very bravely told them the truth, which Shane hadn’t known, nevermind his parents. Ilya hadn’t owed it to them.

It was safe to say that Shane had never wanted to scream at his mother as much as he had in that moment.

Instead of doing so, Shane had cut the visit short, taken Ilya back to the condo, and sat with him on the back patio, rubbing his back as he chain smoked an entire pack of cigarettes in horrifying silence.

The next day when Ilya had gone to the team’s practice facility for his workout, Shane called his mom and had a very sharply worded argument about boundaries and respecting his partner, even though she’d probably learned her lesson: She'd had a front row seat to the man advertised as hockey’s sexy, charming bad boy begging them not to think his mother was weak for taking her own life.

Some indeterminable amount of time later, Ilya’s hand clamped tight around Shane’s ankle, drawing him out of his reverie and back to Derek, who despite his big frame, looked hopelessly small and young on their couch. His face was scrunched in concentration, probably trying to picture how a conversation like that would go for him.

Shane liked Luca, but—

Ilya confirmed Shane's concern as if reading his mind. “Luca does not know better.” He exhaled deeply, like he would if he were having a cigarette. “He is very young and home life is good.” His face did something complicated. “It does not get easier if you ignore it.”

Ilya had a good point. Luca was twenty and bright. His world view was wonderfully optimistic. Derek was a little further ahead in that regard, both in terms of age and experience. He’d experienced horrible things, been forced to make impossible decisions.

Leaning forward to set his phone face down on the coffee table, Ilya lifted his chin, all of it a sign he meant business. “Think it over, yes? You can stay as long as you need. We can talk or watch terrible movies and you can tell me to fuck off at any time,” he continued easily.

Swallowing, Derek shook his head, though he looked surprised that Ilya had been the one to offer, not Shane. “I wouldn’t do that.”

Ilya huffed, halfway amused. “No,” he agreed. “You would not. You probably should have ages ago.”

Derek gave him a half smile that hurt Shane’s chest. He thought back to the summer, to midnight drives and talking about stumbling blindly through the hard parts of their relationship together and wished that Derek had that.

It wasn’t Luca’s fault. In some other universe, or whatever, there was probably some version of Shane who was blissfully ignorant and less capable of supporting Ilya the way he should. Hell, even now Shane worried that he wasn't supportive enough.

If someone were to surgery the room, Shane would be the outlier. He was pretty sure that Ilya would be the more valuable person to talk to about this sort of thing and it might be easier if Shane and his downright picturesque childhood weren’t lording over them. “Same for me,” he offered belatedly. “If, you know—”

“Shane is also too nice for his own good,” Ilya commented dryly. “Stay, please. Unless Derek kicks you out.”

“Pretty sure he knows all my business,” Derek said, setting his mug on its coaster with a small clatter like a punctuation mark.

“Ah, he is very easy to talk to, yes? Even if he looks like he is not reacting, it is all in the eyes.” Ilya gestured at Shane’s face, and Derek nodded. “So,” Ilya continued, when it was clear that they were all staying put, Derek on one couch, Shane and Ilya on the other, and nobody was trying to turn on the TV, “What do you think? You will talk to Luca?”

Derek, to his credit, took his time putting together a response rather than denying Ilya outright.

“I don’t want to ruin this for him. He missed the trip last year because of that shoulder thing, and I wasn’t asked because I was on the verge of getting sent to Bellevue to play in the minors for the first three months of the season.”

“Ah. You do not want to be difficult,” Ilya nodded understandingly. “I could take you on trip as my son. We could make silly social media posts about it. Harris would probably love the content.”

“I’m right here,” Shane cut in, frowning. His arms crossed over his chest. If anyone was going to act as—

“You will have him for mothers trip,” Ilya interrupted his thoughts. “It could be whole bit.”

“No thanks,” Derek said with a grimace. “Maybe it’ll be funny someday, but,” he shook his head. “Not right now.”

“Okay,” Ilya agreed, not broaching the contradictory subject that was Shane’s name in his phone. He patted Shane’s ankle as if to indicate they would discuss it later, like he knew Shane had missed something. “Since talking is out, I give you Hollander-style plan instead: Tell your agent to talk to team. Make them intermediary for communications, yes?” He pursed his lips and gestured shoving an imaginary problem at someone else.

Derek nodded.

“They will handle, tell team not to surprise you with unpleasant topics.” With a sneaky grin, he added, “Damage is done now, but if nothing else it will probably encourage team to talk to agent about resigning you. They are behind. I have already complained to Carlton.”

Derek’s confusion was obvious in his tone, but he gladly took the change of subject. “I’m a RFA in July. It's not exactly a rush.”

“You have not heard of offer sheet? GMs are getting smarter.” Ilya shrugged. “Is called having cake and eating it. They sign you now, you don’t get poached with fancy offer sheet over summer then have to deal with me complaining because my boyfriend is so sad to lose his little protege and is making it my problem.”

“What Ilya’s trying to say,” Shane added patiently, “Is that you’ve got value. As a person and a player. If you tell your agent to talk to the team, they’ll leave you alone about family weekends so you won't have any more surprises like today. As for Luca, that’s up to you. We,” he turned to Ilya, who nodded, a fierce look in his eye, “are a terrible example of how to communicate. It took us thirteen years to be honest with each other.”

Ilya snorted. “That is not where I thought you were going, Hollander, but yes. We are terrible.”

Terribly disgusting, the look on Derek’s face read, but he remained silent.

“Can it, Rozanov,” Shane grunted at him. He returned his focus to Derek, trying to be supportive. “You can, like, learn from our mistakes and whatever. Tell him a little bit—”

“Tell him what you feel comfortable with,” Ilya cut in. “It will probably be messy—”

“Not helping—”

“Yes it is,” Ilya interrupted, “Is honest. It will be messy, Briggsy. People are. Is nothing wrong with protecting yourself.” His eyes hardened. “And if Luca gives you problem or you need space, you come to us. Door is always open. I will talk to him—” Derek looked horrified at the prospect, “If you want me to. No details, just,” he shrugged. “I will tell him is not always easy to be around happy family.”

Derek nodded, but it wasn’t the kind of nod that seemed convincing.

In fact, when Derek had finished his tea and decided he would go, voicing his thanks but avoiding their eyes, Ilya followed him to the door, waving Shane off when he tried to follow. They spoke in quiet tones for several minutes. Shane couldn’t quite make out the words.

When he came back to the couch, Ilya draped himself over Shane and exhaled against his thigh. “This is not going to go well,” he murmured. “Is it?”

Shane’s thumb rubbed Ilya’s cheek. He refused to lie. “Luca knows he doesn’t have family from a throwaway comment from Tampa, last year. That’s all Derek’s said to him about it.”

“Motherfucker,” Ilya said, with feeling. “I know we were that stupid,” he admitted. “Stupider, maybe, but—”

“It’s hard to watch,” Shane finished.

“Yes. It is.”

Chapter 17

December, 2021
Columbus, Ohio


Derek had always been avoidant. For the first few weeks after he’d moved in with Shane back in Sweden, a lot of the time it was as if Shane still lived alone. Derek hung his lone suit but otherwise lived out of his suitcase, as if afraid to occupy space despite landing a contract and making the roster. Getting too comfortable was something that had scared him: something which Shane had only learned when it became clear they got along quite well.

Looking back, it was actually quite fortunate for their relationship that Shane was often uncomfortably blunt, especially when he decided he liked someone. It meant that Shane called him out and asked questions, like why Derek didn't use the perfectly good towels in the linen closet and why Derek avoided him after they lost because it was a team game and everyone had off nights and why he was so skittish after breaking over the decorative vase in the entryway, he'd cleaned up the mess right afterward and nobody’d been injured. When it became clear that Derek needed rules and boundaries to feel secure in their shared space, they sat down, worked through it, and came out stronger.

Five seasons later, his usual tactics might work on a highly distracted Luca, but not Shane. Not Ilya, either.

So when Derek played like a monster in the home stand leading up to the fathers trip, sharp and aggressive and hyperfocused in a way Shane and Ilya both recognized, he couldn’t quite escape their oversight. When he put in long hours at the training facility and arena all week between games, Ilya started staying out with him after practice, pretending he needed to work on a random shooting drill just to make sure Derek wasn't being too stupid or pushing himself in an effort to work off his emotions.

When it became clear he needed something more to chew on, Ilya challenged Derek to a little scrimmage, which was an exercise in learning what Shane had recognized in him years earlier: the kid was versatile and playing nowhere near his ceiling. There was a reason he was playing right around nineteen minutes a night with Shane, why he featured prominently on the team's second power play unit and was trusted to kill penalties if the guys on the third line needed a change. He was a big boy, yet fast and more agile than people expected. Most importantly, he played sharp when he was with sharp players.

Unfortunately, Luca wound up having trouble keeping up with them. It wasn't a skill issue, but one related to experience and tenure, which was a tragic sort of irony. Like Derek, Luca was only in his second MLH season but Derek had been playing pro for far longer. It was an experience gap, a need for time and space to grow. With Derek and Shane heating up, pushing each other—far be it from Shane to avoid rising to an occasion, especially on the ice—their line had become a little lopsided so Wiebe had mixed things up.

Swapping lines with Boodram neither looked nor felt like the demotion it kind of was. Their first and second lines could be whatever they were on paper, Shane knew which of them generated more points on average and it was his second line, thank you very much. Moving Bood down to the second line and Luca up only served to further distract him from what was actually going on.

Six days later, they were in Winnipeg following game one of two of the Fathers Trip—they’d fly to Philadelphia tomorrow afternoon for a fancy dinner and play a one o’clock game the following day. Harris was manic, running around and collecting b-roll footage at a sports bar on the same block as their hotel before the impromptu, unavoidable pub crawl that always cropped up on these themed weekends.

The team had separated into two very distinct groups: the boys with father-figures present and those without, which included Troy, Ilya, Derek, and Shane. The latter group was scattered. Troy had experienced a bit of an uncomfortable situation with his father earlier in the day, which had already been handled. He’d played incredibly tonight, the whole team had, but afterward he’d gone right back to his and Harris’ room to call his mother rather than join the team for drinks.

Ilya, on the other hand, had forced himself to make the rounds, introducing himself to everyone’s guests. He saved Luca’s father, Elias, for last. The man was a warm, softspoken man with the same white-blonde hair and bright eyes as his son. Shane had introduced himself at the airport at the start of the trip, trying and failing to hold Derek’s gaze long enough to see if he needed an excuse to be pulled out of their interaction.

The man was exceptionally kind, which, according to Ilya, probably made it worse. Shane could see the slightly glazed look on Derek’s face as he tried to stay engaged in the conversation without letting his emotions get the better of him.

It didn’t take a genius to tell he hadn’t told Luca a goddamn thing about what was going on. If he had, he was pretty sure Luca would have told him not to force himself to go out on this little pub crawl experience.

Since he hadn't, Ilya was unsubtly dragging him away, citing team business or whatever, ignoring the confused look on Luca’s face, the way his head swiveled to watch them walk away.

“Are you hungry?” He read on Ilya’s lips as they walked toward Shane at the bar. Derek was shaking his head.

“Then we will go to hotel. Is past Shane’s bedtime,” he said, looking up at Shane with mirth in his eyes.

“You don’t seem to complain about going to bed early,” Shane chirped back. “I let you watch TV in bed.”

“Wow,” Derek muttered. “So wild.”

“Can it, Bigrig.” Shane snipped, heatless.

“Yes. Wild is for the sex, Briggsy,” Ilya reassured him. “Boring is for everything else.”

“Ugh,” grumbled Shane, “Stop talking about our sex life.”

“Make me,” Ilya said, even though he didn’t say anything else.

Shane rolled his eyes. “We’re going to watch reruns of Love It Or List It while I bitch about the housing market. He fucking loves it. Don't let him fool you.”

“Fine, fine,” Ilya waved a hand. “Is true.” He pursed his lips and revised, “Unless Masterchef is on.”

“What?”

“You know, show about amateur chefs with Gordon Ramsey. Is only time he isn’t total asshole.”

Shane huffed. “It’s his second favorite Ramsey show, but he’s not allowed to watch Kitchen Nightmares in my presence because he knows I’ll never go out to eat with him ever again if I see what restaurant kitchens actually look like.”

He shuddered as Ilya tittered quietly into his shoulder, a combination brought on by the thought of watching that disgusting show and the cold outside.

“Anyway, our room has two beds, and we only need one as long as Ilya doesn’t bring up our sex life again. You could text Luca that you’re having a sleepover. Give him and his dad some bonding time.”

“Yes, whose idea was it to have the dads share a room with their kids? I know owners are rich. Seems very cheap,” Ilya tapped his cheek consideringly.

Derek crossed his arms. His tone was suspicious. “I already have my own room for this exact reason.”

“So? We aren’t fucking tonight. Did that this morning—”

“Ilya, I swear to god—”

“—so you take the extra bed. Is stupid to be alone tonight and you know it.”

Derek bit his lip, unsure.

They made it back to the hotel and parted ways, which they’d have to do no matter what, given all of Derek’s belongings were in his room. Ilya exhaled frustratedly at Derek’s silence but didn’t push.

“We’re in two-two-three,” Shane told him, thumb jerking in their room’s direction when they parted ways. “Seriously, come hang out.”

He didn’t. Shane waited up until one just in case, but Derek had decided to spend the night alone.


—


“Derek's avoiding me.”

Shane, despite flinching at the sharp interruption piercing his very comfortable silence in the team’s weight room, did not falter, stacking his weight over his left leg high on the box before hopping back down. He was at rep seven of ten on the right leg, he told himself mentally. He’d done the left side first.

“Hello to you too, Luca.”

“Shit. Sorry, Shane.” Luca went over to the rack and picked up a kettlebell.

He didn’t seem particularly interested in doing anything other than holding it, so Shane went back to the box and finished the last three reps before picking up the box and putting it back in its place in the corner.

“Do you know where he is?”

Shane plucked the kettlebell from Luca’s hand since he wasn’t using it and he needed it for the next exercise in his workout, as well as the shortest box in the set. “He’s with Ilya at the Humane Society.”

Frowning, Luca blurted, “Why?”

Deciding not to answer, he said, “Set me a timer, would you?”

“What?”

“Timer,” Shane pressed. “One minute, please.”

“Ask Siri?”

“My phone’s in my bag. Yours isn’t. Time me, and I’ll time you. We can swap.”

Giving Luca a task was the right choice. When Shane focused on moving side to side, shifting hands on the kettlebell so fast it seemed like it was barely moving, he could tell Luca had locked in. Guys loved to work on strength, but Shane had found that pylometrics and mobility were more useful than a PR at the squat rack. He finished the set and traded places with Luca, who handed over his phone.

Another minute later, breathing a little unsteady, Luca asked, “Isn’t that loud and um, dirty? From all the animals? That doesn’t seem like his thing at all.”

It wasn’t, Shane supposed. Ilya went whenever he had a free day, even if it was only for an hour or two. He didn’t make a big deal about it, and while his visits did make their way onto social media it was more organic: a shaky video of Ilya with an older dog asking Ottawans to open their home for Mister Bubbles or whatever crazy name the volunteers came up with. Shane was all in favor of helping animals in need, but unlike Ilya, he couldn’t handle the sensory overload that came with the shelter. He was pretty sure that it was going to be a point used against him once Ilya found a dog he wanted to bring home with him for real.

Ilya would probably bring home every dog he saw if Shane let him, but Shane planned to negotiate for one which would probably wind up meaning less than three. At least he’d agreed to table pet discussions until the summer, when they were home and able to commit time to training.

But anyways, Derek.

“Ilya invited him.”

“Why?” Luca wondered aloud. “He’s always inviting me to stuff, not Derek.”

Was Luca jealous? Shane tried to figure it out by looking at Luca’s face, but even with eye contact he couldn’t tell. “Aren’t your family here to visit?”

“Yeah, but we could’ve all gone together. Ilya said they always need volunteers, especially this time of year. My sister loves animals.” He made a face. “Derek never skips optional skate and he also never hangs out with Roz. They kind of famously had a whole big argument last year about, you know.”

Yeah, Shane thought. “I mean, we’re all friends now. We did the whole double date thing in August. The brunches, or whatever. You guys come over all the time.”

“I guess.” Luca didn’t seem convinced.

Shane motioned Luca toward the line of rowers that looked out over the parking lot from three floors above.

Reluctantly, Luca trailed along behind him. “But you do know he’s avoiding me, don’t you? He has been since the fathers trip.” He frowned. “Not obviously. He still answers my messages and we live together, you know? It's just, it’s like he's ghosting me, even though he's right there. He promised me he wouldn't do that after last year, and I feel like he's found a way around it somehow.”

Focusing on the motion of rowing gave Shane a couple seconds to put together his thoughts and decide that talking while using the rowing machine was kind of a pain in the ass. He should’ve chosen the bike instead. “I think,” he exhaled slowly and started again. “I think you should talk to Derek if you have concerns about your relationship.”

“I’m trying. He’s been telling me that everything’s fine. My parents think he doesn’t like them or something,” he murmured. “They already have thoughts about how much older he is.”

Well, shit. “Did they say this in front of him?”

“Not really. They kind of hinted at it? My sister thought it was funny, but not in a mean way. I told them to knock it off, though.”

“Right.” Shane resisted the truly overwhelming urge to roll his eyes. Derek and Shane were alike in their overthinking and anxious tendencies, but Derek was better at hiding his anxiety than he was at pretending like he didn’t avoid any whiff of confrontation. He was hypersensitive to potential problems. There was no way in hell he hadn’t noticed and taken it personally. “Look, I’m not really the person to go to for relationship advice, kid.”

“Maybe not,” Luca allowed, “But you know him better than anyone. He has literally said you’re family to him.”

And that was the rub, wasn’t it? It wasn’t like Derek expected Shane to step in and fulfill any role, jokey contact labels be damned, but there was a shred of truth in there. Shane was what Derek had, what he’d kind of chosen for himself after proximity had brought them together.

He slowed to a stop on the rower and Luca followed suit beside him.
