The words he tried to put together were clumsy in his mind and even worse held behind his teeth. “You’re right,” he began, looking Luca in the eye. “And he’d be furious with me if I told you his business, which is why I’m telling you to talk to him.”

“I’m trying,” insisted Luca. “He keeps lying and saying everything’s okay but it’s like he’s trying to keep busy to stay away from my family and I can’t keep playing intermediary.”

“Have you told him that?”

“Of course not. I don’t want to fight with him.”

Privately, Shane doubted they would fight. Derek would rather internalize his feelings until he could ignore them. He kept that to himself.

“Ilya and I never talked about, well,” he shrugged. “Anything, really. We’d accidentally hurt each other over and over again because we were too stubborn and scared to talk to each other. So if you want advice—which you clearly do,” he looked Luca up and down, “I’m telling you to try. Be patient with him, if you can, and if you can’t, that’s,” he shrugged. “You guys are young—” when Luca bristled, he shook his head. “No, I mean, the both of you are. Not everybody meets the guy they want to spend the rest of their lives with as a teenager, and even if they do it doesn’t mean it’s destined to work out.”

Shane met Luca’s eyes again and startled. They were wet. “What the fuck?”

“Shit,” He tried not to match Luca’s now obvious panic. “I don’t mean that your relationship is doomed or anything, I just,” he looked up to the ceiling, trying to force his thoughts into something useful. “I’m so terrible at this,” he admitted. “What I’m trying to say is that you should try and hear him out when he’s ready to be heard. If you know something’s bothering him, all you can do is try and be understanding, whatever it is. But if you’ve got concerns, you also need to tell him about them. Neither of you can like, read minds or whatever, so you have to talk to each other.”


—


“So.”

Shane looked up, over the top of his iPad, glasses on. Ilya had just come in from the garage. He wasn’t smiling. Almost immediately, Shane’s free hand landed on his knee, fingers tapping against the top of the joint in an attempt to self-soothe: one two three four five, five four three two one.

“What happened?”

“Luca called me,” Ilya said carefully, kicking off his boots, which he placed on the drying mat next to the rug.

Shane took off his glasses and only just resisted the impulse to bite on the plastic guard that was meant to keep the edge of the frame arms from digging into his skin. “Oh fuck,” he breathed. “I fucked up, didn’t I?”

Ilya frowned. “What? No.” He shoved his gloves into his pockets and hung his jacket on the hook next to the garage door. “Luca called because he and his family showed up at Humane Society unannounced to volunteer and Derek did not answer his phone.”

“Fuck. I told him to talk to Derek, not sic his family on him.” He shoved off his usual stool at the counter. “How bad is it?”

“Bad.” He sighed. “I am going to take shower because I was helping them bathe big dogs who need help getting into tub and you hate wet animal smell. Come upstairs?”

“After you,” he said. He caught a little bit of that wet fur smell Ilya was talking about, but at least he didn’t smell like dog piss again. It was the only downside of Ilya sending him pictures covered in puppies, in Shane’s opinion.

Ilya didn’t speak again until he’d stripped naked and stepped in the shower, Shane taking his clothes to the washer downstairs before coming back up with a fresh towel for the warming rack. “Derek is trying too hard,” he told Shane. “It makes me angry at Luca.”

“It’s not his fault.”

“I know this.” Ilya aggressively pumped body wash onto Shane’s loofah without so much as a comment about how loofahs were for old people, even though he’d taken to using it daily.

Shane leaned against the bathroom sink and waited.

“How do you live with someone and not notice this is difficult subject? He does not have family, he does not talk about family—”

“Luca literally just told me he was talking to me because I’m the closest thing Derek has to family.”

“Fuck,” Ilya groaned. “How is he so smart on the ice and such a fucking idiot?”

“Technically the frontal lobe of our brains doesn’t fully develop until we’re in our mid to late twenties,” Shane pointed out. “It takes longer for men.”

Ilya flicked water out of the shower at him. “What the fuck? How is that helpful?”

“It’s literally unhelpful,” Shane countered blandly. “That’s the point I’m making.”

“Ugh. Enough talk of brains.” Ilya turned off the water and held a hand out for his towel. Shane chucked it at his head instead, but it only earned him a subtle crack of a smile and not the whole thing. “Luca’s family does not leave until the night of the twenty-fifth. We have another week of this.”

There was something else too. “Did Derek say anything to you about things?”

“He said he knows he is making it awkward.”

“Nothing about being too old for Luca?”

“Luca is young, yes, but he is professional hockey player, not a child.”

“I know.” Shane watch Ilya half heartedly dry his curls. “His family said something about the age gap, which Luca insists he smoothed over, but—”

“But Derek is very sensitive about answering his boyfriend’s family’s concerns because he wants to make good impression.”

They held eye contact for a bit too long. “Fuck,” Shane said, catching on to the parallel. “I’m sorry—”

“No no no, none of that.” Ilya looped the towel over the rack to dry and bullied himself into Shane’s personal space. “Is over now. Yuna apologized and we talked it out, we are good.” He pressed a kiss to the tip of Shane’s nose, watched his face scrunch, sensitive. “Luca is mama’s boy, like you, but,” he hissed out a breath. “Is not—you did not feel like you had to choose, right?”

“It wasn’t a choice.”

Ilya thunked their foreheads together. “I hate you. You are Mr Romantic. Always perfect words.”

“I don’t know who you’re confusing me with, but that’s definitely not true.” Shane kissed his forehead, then led him out into their cooler bedroom, pulling out Ilya’s usual loungewear and tossing it at the bed. “I fuck up all the time, as you know. I’ve been putting spinach in your smoothies for weeks.”

“I knew they tasted worse than usual!” Ilya bleated.

“Shut up, you need to eat more vegetables.” He grabbed one of his own hole-riddled and stretched-out hoodies to finish the ensemble.

Ilya snatched it up, bringing the front of it to his face like he’d remembered Shane had worn it the other day but not long enough for it to be considered dirty, seeking out traces of his cologne. He must have found some because he sagged a little, comforted. Shane understood. He was wearing one of Ilya’s hoodies himself.

“Anyway, not to go back to my accidental romantic gesture, but—”

“I already gave you credit,” Ilya pointed out, then held his hands up in surrender when Shane glared at him.

“Do you ever think that we’d have been fucked if we got together sooner?” Shane bit his lip. “Not 2017, but like, earlier than that. If I’d gotten my shit together.”

“We never would have. I was not ready.”

“I know that, and neither was I, but…”

Ilya seemed to think about it. “It would have been a disaster. It was bad enough fucking each other semi-regularly and having tree feelings—”

“Tree feelings,” Shane repeated dubiously.

“You know, the big—” he mimed out a triangle with an inarticulate sound. “Pine trees,” he explained. “Pining.”

Shane couldn’t help himself. He yanked Ilya’s face down just enough to kiss him hard on the lips. “I fucking love you,” he said, smiling softly as they parted. He shook his head almost disbelievingly. “Tree feelings. You’re such a weirdo.”

“Ah, but I am your weirdo, yes? What does that say about you?”

“So many things,” he admitted, letting Ilya wrap him up in an embrace just to send them toppling onto the bed.

Ilya could get dressed later.

Chapter 18

Chapter Notes

Writing update: I'm working on the final arc of the story! Monday updates will continue for the foreseeable future but I am plugging away.

January, 2022
Los Angeles, CA


Derek and Luca seemed to get their shit together around Christmas, coincidentally around the time Luca's family returned to Europe. Seemed was the correct word, Shane thought, because Derek very staunchly did not speak another word on the subject after telling Shane that he and Luca were fine, status quo restored.

So while the rapid resolution still nagged at them—Ilya especially, for whatever reason, Shane was content to let it lie. Ilya was correct in reminding them both that they couldn’t fight the kids’ battles for them, and they quickly found themselves distracted by the passing of the season and the upcoming All Star Break.

The whole thing was a lot less dramatic than Shane had been expecting.

It wasn’t some horrible, vindictive revenge plot by the league. He wasn’t getting sidled with captaining a team full of his former Montreal teammates or being excluded because of some secret clause in his settlement—he’d read all one hundred seventy eight pages of legalese that had come out of mediation and memorized most of it.

Instead it was all business: he’d been excluded from the initial selection because the Writers Association had only been allowed to vote for players who had participated in the previous season. Shane had been relegated to the fan vote for one of the twelve remaining spots.

It was all boringly—in the derogatory sense—standard. Teams would be “randomly” selected to the League's satisfaction and the game would be played three on three like glorified pond hockey. The guys who were invited voted for who they wanted to captain, and Ilya won, because of course he did. When it became clear that Shane would be top twelve in voting, he was immediately confirmed as the first fan-voted addition to the other team—captained by Carter Vaughn—and given a letter.

The Centaurs' locker room thought this was hilarious.

“Wise decision by the league,” Chewie joked with reporters in a post practice scrum. “We were worried they were becoming too friendly.”

“I talked to Hayden Pike, actually,” Wyatt said, before a home game against Boston. “He’s really excited about playing on a line with Rozanov.”

Ilya had screamed across the room about lies and slander, which had the media—and thus the fans—laughing about it for a week. It made the rounds in the group chat with the added point that he’d been bare assed when Wyatt had not so accidentally eviscerated him.

To be fair, Hayden was looking forward to playing with him. He’d never admit it, but Shane could tell. They were talking in the group chat about lines way too much for people who didn’t care, and like Shane, Wyatt also tended to lurk in the chat and react when Svetlana ripped on someone for a stupid comment.

The local media wouldn’t be going out to LA for the weekend, but seemed content with Shane’s usual, “I’m looking forward to it. It’s a great weekend for the fans and I’m grateful to have their votes and be able to participate.”

It was true. Shane liked All Star Weekend. It was just enough time to mingle with other players and teams without experiencing massive burnout. An added bonus now, as opposed to before, was that he was mostly unplugged from a lot of the goings. Ilya was, of course, a well-informed gossip magnet, but he mostly enjoyed dunking on stupid decisions and only occasionally provided actual updates relayed to him by veterans across the league.

Actually, there were other bonuses, too. He didn’t have a million brand deals to appease, extra spots or interviews lined up for him by CCM or Reebok or whatever crappy tasting electrolyte they pretended was in the water bottles with their logo on them but was actually water or gatorade. The League wanted to drum up drama by pitting him and Ilya against one another, and that was… fine, honestly.

In their final scrum before the trip, Ilya vowed to the reporters that he would score at least five goals on Hazy despite having Hayden on his line, to which Hazy said Shane would score a double hat trick, which wasn’t really a thing but would be cool to do anyway.

Shane had likewise doubled down, vowing to send Wyatt an Edible Arrangement that looked like Captain America’s shield if he managed to shut Ilya out—a very tall order given the game format, but it got Wyatt weirdly fired up. Ilya had complained that that was unfair, but he was grinning ear to ear as he said it.

After all, the All Star Game was supposed to be fun for everybody, wasn’t it?


—


“Remind me to give you my room number in front of Hunter tomorrow for old time’s sake,” Ilya said as they made their way down to the hotel restaurant for breakfast.

Shane shoved him into the elevator. “We’re in the same room.”

The doors closed behind them and Shane smacked Ilya’s hand away from the elevator buttons. They would be going to the rooftop restaurant, not the one in the lobby. Hayden and Cliff were probably already waiting.

“So? I want to see if he noticed all those years ago.” With nothing to do and his hand outstretched, Ilya grabbed his ass instead, pinching it. “Like science experiment.”

“You’re such a menace.”

“I am going to have to pretend to be your best friend for our adoring public,” Ilya said to his reflection in the mirrored doors of the elevator. The cab hummed as it began its descent. “Your ass will be lonely.”

“My ass will be just fine because you will get to see it tonight—” Ilya perked up, “If you behave.”

He immediately deflated. “No. This is impossible task. I am on a team with Hayden and Scott fucking Hunter—”

“And you will behave or I will sleep in my hotel room alone.”

Ilya shook his head ruefully, like Shane had told a hilarious joke. “You’re a terrible fucking liar, malysh. You will get all bashful about sneaking into my hotel room—”

“Will I?” Shane raised his eyebrows, which seemed to be a new thing that got Ilya hot and bothered. Maybe it was the combination of his chirping and the eyebrows. Could also be the note of challenge in his voice. Either way, “Maybe I’ll go up first and get ready for you.” He pursed his lips and gestured between them with his hand as if weighing his options. “Or I could pack up my toys and go—”

Ilya looked at the little screen on the wall that displayed the floor they were on, decided to risk it and shoved Shane against the wall, kissing him fiercely. “You are a terrible person.”

“Maybe,” Shane grinned, grinding their pelvises together.

Ilya nipped at his throat and muttered, a little breathless, “Definitely. I am going to do terrible—” the cab dinged to signal it was stopping several floors early. They jumped apart as the doors opened, Ilya trying and failing to casually readjust himself, Shane's cheeks splotched color that made his freckles even more obvious.

“Jesus Christ,” Scott Hunter growled, stubble dark on his jaw and eyes dead in the way of one who desperately required coffee. He stepped into the elevator car, evaluated their destination and jabbed the button that read ‘lobby’ as if he'd rather be anywhere they weren't. “You two have never been subtle, have you?”

“Pretty sure that’s just your gaydar, Hunter,” Shane said dryly.

He wouldn't pretend like he had gaydar himself, but a lot of queer people did. Also, straight people—men, especially—were allergic to noticing the most obvious things. Ilya had been in Shane's lap on Halloween, kissing him, and the guys had thought it was a prank, for fuck’s sake. A gay man would notice because he knew what to look for, obviously. Shane thought so at least.

“Oh my god, Hollander,” Ilya guffawed, swatting at Shane’s shoulder. “This is going to be the best fucking weekend.”

Scott sighed and hung his head.


—


The media was given what was, according to the very bubbly League handler who stuck to Shane like glue, the most robust press sheet outlining topics Shane would not be covering in interviews.

Shane had taken a peek at it over her shoulder, because, the woman was five feet with shoes on and Shane was head and shoulders taller than her. They would have been better putting what Shane could talk about on it.

Mostly, the media wanted to know about the rivalry.

Shane had given them several sound bites about how the rivalry was alive and well—hadn’t they seen what happened in October? As for Shane’s thoughts on playing against him, he’d touched on how Ilya was such a special player and one he’d loved playing against and was looking forward to putting in his place for old times’ sake, how the guys back in Ottawa had placed bets on who would score more points, with the proceeds going to a charity of the winners choice in the city and he looked forward to winning.

Otherwise, he kept his points relatively light, briefly commenting about how important it was for inclusion and visibility to have queer players on each of the teams before questions pivoted towards the reception in the room. He mentioned the warm reception he received from guys like Marleau and Vaughn while leaving out the couple of guys who avoided him as if they could catch homosexuality like a disease.

Nobody dared say anything to him directly, probably afraid of Shane wielding lawyers like a weapon—because nothing discouraged outward bigotry and discrimination in rich people quite like the threat of legal action—but there were guys on the team (Comeau, Gagnon, some nobody defenseman from Florida) who favored loaded glances and uptight silences in Shane's general vicinity. The good outweighed the bad, and whomever was handling the Montreal contingent had clearly told them to play neutral if they couldn't play nice.

And of course, Cliff was almost as much of a shadow as the tiny PR wrangler, which Shane had asked Ilya about afterward. Ilya denied it, but Hayden had told on him almost immediately, the two of them bent over a dry erase board stolen from one of the assistant coaches. Hayden mostly said it to take back control of the dry erase marker Ilya was hogging while they waited for their meals in the Chilis built into the hotel lobby.

“I mean, maybe I beat up Roz to defend your honor, Hollzy,” Cliff said slyly over Ilya's disgruntled outburst about the hockey gods torturing him with a team full of goody two shoes and tattle tales.

“I should have taken fine for skipping,” Ilya grumbled. “First you beat me in speed competition—”

“Told you I would,” Shane gloated.

“ —Now you are sending your new teammates to beat me up…”

“You are full of shit,” Shane huffed. “I heard you and Cliff pre-arranging a fight last week. You didn’t even try to be subtle about it.”

“We’re giving the fans what they want!” and “We haven't fought each other since October of 2018,” they complained over each other in tandem, which sent Hayden into a fit of laughter.

“The last one was less orchestrated, da?” Svetlana rounded out their little group. She had seated herself between Ilya and Shane in the corner booth, which was made for eight people but barely sat four hockey players and one woman comfortably. He pulled his legs closer to himself because he was pretty sure that was Cliff's leg he'd been touching.

“I was angry,” Cliff agreed. “Sue me.”

“I have a reputation now,” Shane remarked. “Don't tempt me.” Everyone erupted into laughter around him.

Svetlana's long fingernails bit into his shoulder as she shook it to indicate just how funny she'd found his words. She'd been surprised at his dry humor when they met in November, the way it contradicted the buttoned up, media trained version of Shane people knew from scrums and scattered interviews.

“I needed a good punch in the face to get my head on straight, Marley. And you were not trying to commit murder anyway.”

“I wasn't pulling punches—”

“No, I know,” Ilya ripped the marker from Hayden’s hand, “You broke my nose.”

Hands cupped around her mouth, Svetlana whispered into his ear, “You will need to control them, Hollander—”

“Anyone with a black eye or broken nose from fighting will be uninvited to go out with Rose after the game,” he told them.

Boos erupted, as was to be expected.

Marleau gestured to Shane and Svetlana though he’d turned to better face Ilya. “This team-up is fucked, brother. It's over. We’re never gonna win again.”

Hayden chuckled to himself.

“What is so funny, Pike?”

He was typing rapidly on his phone, but stopped and looked up long enough to answer. “Jacks is so pissed off she’s missing this. Chicken pox 2.0 is ruining our house, so I’ve been giving her the play-by-play.”

“She wanted to meet Rose?”

“I mean, yeah,” Hayden shrugged, “But also child-free weekend and whatever shenanigans come from your side of the table.”

“I would have taken her shopping,” Ilya said, sighing. It quickly became a cough when Svetlana's elbow dug into his ribs. “We,” he revised, gasping. “Sveta, please.”

“Rose and I were going to go shopping tomorrow after the game, before we go out,” Shane mentioned. He was blowing off the league after-party for it. “You can come with us if you want.”

“Shane Hollander,” she drawled, turning to him as if her tiny frame could block out Ilya's plaintive whining.

“Nooo, Hollander, I want to go shopping too,” Ilya complained.

Shane was struck by how much he liked hearing his name spoken by someone with a Russian accent, even if Ilya was being overly dramatic.

Svetlana continued, “You are speaking my language.”

“This is betrayal,” Ilya lamented.

“Dude,” Hayden had abandoned messaging Jackie to flick condensation from his drink onto the dry erase board. He wiped it most of the way clean with a napkin. “Let them go shopping. Trust me.”

“Since when the fuck do I do that?” Ilya demanded.

Hayden’s raised eyebrows and thin lipped smile that said he knew exactly when trust between them had started. “Did Shane pack an outfit for the club?” he asked.

Ilya exhaled sharply.

“Thought so,” Hayden smirked. “Let them go.”

“You are so down bad, Rozy,” Cliff cut in, laughing.

“I hate you all,” pouted Ilya.

“Aww,” Svetlana reached over to ruffle Ilya’s messy curls. “Poor baby.”

Chapter 19

Chapter Notes

This chapter is one of my favorites.

Shane was pulled by the hand into the club, people making sounds of awe and recognition all around him—mostly in Rose’s direction, thankfully. It was Svetlana who tugged him along, Rose at her side, the two of them with sinister grins on their faces as they stared down the pulsing lights and heavy thrum of bass as though surveying their kingdom. It really wasn’t Shane’s scene. He knew it was theirs, though, and he had spent a good chunk of his twenties going to clubs and pretending like he fit in there.

It was a little easier now when it wasn’t really expected of him and his presence was treated like the rarity it was. Ilya, Cliff, and Hayden had arrived earlier, heading up to the VIP area Rose’s people had arranged for them. He always gave her shit for it, but if they hadn’t, he would’ve called Farah and had whoever managed him on her behalf do the same.

His phone buzzed as he let himself be dragged to the bar on the upper level, the one away from the casual club-goers. He checked it when his hand was released.

MLH ALERT: Shane Hollander has been fined $10,000, the maximum allowed under the collective bargaining agreement, for failure to comply with post-game media procedures following Sunday afternoon’s All Star Game.

“Something wrong?”

Sveta was leaning over the bar and gesturing high up, no doubt to where the best vodka was kept out of reach. It had been Rose that spoke.

Shane pocketed his phone and looked Rose in the eye. They narrowed as he shook his head, eyebrows pulled in closer together. She was good at reading people—it was part of why she could work with literally any actor in Hollywood and beyond, why she was one of the masters of her craft.

“Reallly. I’m good.”

She ducked her chin. “It’s not Ilya, is it? I know you said he might be a little,” she trailed off, making a face Shane couldn’t quite translate into a mood or action.

“No, um, it’s nothing. Work drama,” he summarized. That’s what non-hockey people called this sort of thing, right?

“Okay,” she supposed. “Though work drama for you is something entirely different, all things considered. ”

He thumbed at his lower lip. “I assume someone caught wind of us shopping in Beverly Hills,” he said, motioning to his shirt, which Rose had actually bought for him in Rio while doing reshoots for some X-Force spinoff-slash-sequel over the holidays and gifted to him earlier. “I got fined for blowing off post game media.”

“I thought you said you were let go.”

“Apparently the league felt differently,” he shrugged. It must have been because he’d showered and left before the media cleared out of the locker room. There was a thirty minute window after games where the media was expected to be in the room, but not that every guy was expected to speak every game and Shane had done press the day before. “Farah will handle it.”

She held his gaze for a long moment, searching for something. If he was sure, maybe. Shane was. While he did try his best to remain humble, losing ten thousand dollars was pocket change to him. He’d just spent way more money in the span of a couple hours.

Rose ducked her head. “Shane?” A hand was waved in front of his face. “Hello?”

He shook his head to reset. “Sorry, I was thinking about something else.” He paused, holding her gaze again, realizing he must have looked away when he’d gotten caught up in his head. “It’s fine. Anyway—”

Sveta reinserted herself, handing them both a shot glass and ending the conversation. “Quickly, so we can tell Ilya we started without him,” she wore a cunning grin.

They clinked glasses and drank.

The vodka was cold, which was about the only good thing about it. Shane didn’t enjoy shots, but they were better than spending an hour half-heartedly sipping at a drink he hated given to him by someone intending to be polite. Sveta plucked their glasses out of their hands and they were moving again. The floor buzzed with the thrum of bass from a song he didn’t know, and the lights on the dancefloor to his right were simultaneously neon bright and dark.

Shane felt his shoulders drop as Ilya came into view. The club was hot and humid from all the bodies which made Ilya’s curls a little more defined, but Shane had noticed his shirt first: It was black, nearly see-through, and gauzy with brown and white abstract shapes on it in no conceivable pattern as far as Shane could tell. Ilya was laughing, his head tipped back and one arm slung around Cliff.

Hayden noticed them first, waving to Shane and gesturing to the not quite private alcove that had been reserved for them, but Ilya rose, shuffling around Cliff’s massive legs to loom before them.

“Hello Ilya,” Rose greeted, extending a hand.

She hadn't even given him time to admire Shane's outfit, which she'd picked out.

“Rose Landry,” he drawled, unimpressed. With a raised eyebrow, he looked down at it, then back up at her face. He begrudgingly wrapped his massive hand around hers and shook it. Greeting done, he set about appraising Shane’s pale purple shirt, which lacked sleeves and the ability to cover a strip of his lower abdomen, and well-tailored pants. One eyebrow lowered to its usual height but the other kept its elegant lifted arch. “You look pretty.”

Shane rolled his eyes. “Please be nice to my friend,” he grumbled, flushing under the weight of Ilya’s attention. “She’s been looking forward to meeting you.”

“Do not ruin my friendship with Rose, Ilya,” Sveta insisted, kissing his cheek as she snuck between them to take Ilya’s seat beside Cliff, who tasked himself with flagging down a roving server.

Rose laughed. “You literally can’t,” she promised, “And also I can say with complete certainty that I will win Ilya over. I have stories about Shane getting drunk on box wine. I will barter them to earn your friendship.”

Flinging his arms out, Shane gestured between the two of them as he deftly scooted away. “See? You both love to embarrass me. Please bond about it while I hang out with Hayden.”


—


To his credit, Ilya didn’t immediately seek Shane out the second he’d established a truce—Shane was barely convinced that was the correct word for it but their interactions seemed non-hostile so he’d take it as a win. Instead, he pulled Svetlana onto the dancefloor, Cliff following along with a backwards glance at Rose, who waved him off with a serene smile and disappeared into the roiling throng of bodies.

“You could not pay me to do that,” Hayden said, shaking his head ruefully as he watched them go.

Rose gave him a funny look. “Really? Shane told me you met your wife at a place like this.”

Pulled back into the conversation by name, Shane was rewarded with the sight of Hayden’s face going blank, then sad. “Oh my god, I’m an old man,” he lamented pitifully. “Shane, please dance with me—”

“Not on your life,” Shane replied instantly. “Rose?”

“Sorry,” she winced. “You would be accused of cheating on your wife so fast,” she looked between herself and Hayden. “Actually, you should probably move further away. I swear the internet thinks anyone I breathe around is actually a secret lover.”

“Sounds terrible.”

“I mean,” she rolled her eyes skyward, “Obviously, yes, I’m very lucky to be a successful as I am, I don’t, like, want you to think I’m an ungrateful bitch—”

“Shane would never be friends with someone like that,” Hayden pointed out. He leaned forward, setting his drink down. “So? Did everything go good on your little shopping trip?”

Rose’s entire face lit up.

“Yeah,” Shane said, looking out at the dance floor. He thought he caught sight of Ilya’s curls, but the strobe lights made it difficult. “Mission accomplished.”

Hayden sighed. “Buddy,” he drawled. “That’s it? That’s all you’re going to give me?”

“Svetlana punched him—”

“Oh shit—”

“In the shoulder! Jesus, Rose,” Shane shook his head. A server came by to collect the abandoned glasses on their table, Hayden making a circle with his finger to insinuate refills were in order.

When the server left, Rose went on. “It was really sweet. She was so surprised.”

Shane failed to resist the urge to roll his eyes. “Are you going to date Svetlana or something? She might agree if you offer her your closet.”

“Oh, she would definitely agree,” Hayden said. “Between her and my wife, I've been hearing about your style for weeks.”

“I wish,” Rose huffed. “Being straight is my most toxic trait.”

Shane laughed. Only Rose would find being straight to be challenging. “I think your type not being straight men is your most toxic trait,” he corrected.

“Ugh!” She reached her fingers into her drink, fished out an ice cube, and threw it at him. “You’re literally the worst gay best friend,” she said. “I’m going to have to promote Miles.”

“As long as you don’t call him to come out.” Shane was instantly on guard. “I know Ilya is… prickly—”

“Like a very angry cactus, yes,” Rose interjected.

“But he’s trying. I don’t think he’d handle Miles well at all.”

“Considering Miles never got over you,” Rose waved a hand. “I would never do that to either of them. Miles is an idiot himbo—I love him to pieces, but he’s got no self preservation whatsoever. He’d probably want to sleep with both of you—”

“Absolutely not.”

“Oh my God,” Hayden leaned in, recognizing the name-dropped celebrity being mentioned. “You’re talking about Miles—”

“Yes,” Both he and Rose hissed in unison.

“Shane, buddy, I know you’re not on the market, but I need to appreciate your game—”

“On the ice, and nowhere else,” A very sharp, unamused Ilya said from behind them. He had a bottle tucked under his arms and two stacks of glasses.

“You guys weren’t gone long.”

“I figure I need to get Shane drunk if he’s going to dance,” Ilya said playfully, even though Shane had told him at least ten times he would not be dancing.

Hayden, who didn’t need a conversation to understand that Shane would rather be bag skated than endure one song on a dancefloor, gave Ilya a pointed look and cut directly to the heart of the issue. “You know you have nothing to worry about, right?” He said carefully.

“Mm, yes,” Ilya set the bottle down on the table and distributed shot glasses, then wiped condensation from the bottle off on Hayden’s sleeve without so much as a smirk. “I know this logically, yes,” he dropped onto the cushion beside Shane, their thighs touching, “But this takes longer to catch up,” he tapped at his chest and looked up at Rose. “Is nothing personal.”

“God, you’re such a dad, Hayd,” Shane cut in, unwilling to let Ilya explain himself further. He was trying. That was enough.

And Rose, because she’d always been good at people, accepted this with a short nod and immediately changed the subject. “Shots?”

“Shots,” Ilya agreed. He poured hers first.


—


Unlike Rose, Shane was not a giggly drunk when crowds were involved. She was still laughing, cackling loudly—like a hyena, Svetlana had said just moments earlier—at something Cliff and Hayden were arguing about. There were a lot of empty shot glasses. The one in front of Shane still had a little left in the bottom of it, a couple drops he’d failed to drain even though he couldn’t feel the burn of the liquor anymore.

Shane was more likely to withdraw when drunk in social situations like this one. The haze of intoxication made it easier to tune out the more overwhelming things that grated upon his senses: the lights, the wall of noise, being surrounded on all sides by people, even in the VIP area, but the tradeoff was that he was more likely to lose himself in the slow, sluggish stream of his thoughts.

Take right now for example: Shane was not quite snuggled up against Ilya, but there were several points of contact. Shane’s right side touched Ilya’s left at the ankle, knee and thigh. Ilya’s left arm was slung across the back of the lounger, his fingers occasionally brushing Shane’s left shoulder at random, his arm a warm bar across Shane’s shoulderblades otherwise. Shane liked that warmth. The more rational voice in his head—the one that was easily ignorable in this pleasantly bleary state which he would likely regret tomorrow—had lingering concerns about his IQ being drained away by the steady stream of alcohol because he usually didn’t let himself get too caught up in sensations outside of the bedroom, but—

“Are you drunk enough to dance with me yet, kotik?”

His head tipped sideways, the rest of his upper body following with it, increasing the pressure of Ilya’s arm against his back. “Still no,” he said thickly, not quite slurring his words.

Ilya grinned, and drew a circle in mid air with his pointer finger. “Pour, Marley.”

“This is coercion,” Shane protested, even though Cliff hadn't moved yet.

“Drink or dance,” Ilya insisted.

“Mm, but I’m so bad at it,” he gestured at Rose, “Tell him, Rose. I’m such a bad dancer.”

“You might be better at it with a man?”

Sveta laughed, which sent Cliff and Hayden into giggles of their own. Ilya squeezed his shoulder.

“Ugh, you all suck.” He leaned forward and popped the top off the bottle of vodka himself. It rolled onto the floor near Hayden’s foot. Shane felt his brow furrow, his forehead feeling a little tingly, which was a bad sign for his future self. He poured them—himself and Ilya, because the rest of them had perfectly working hands—each a shot, some of the liquid sloshing over the rim of the glass from his sloppy aim. “Drink, Rozanov,” he growled, kicking his heel against Ilya’s.

“Yes dear,” Ilya’s eyes were a little too bright, the only sign that he was drunk. It was frankly unfair, Shane thought to himself.

They clinked their glasses together, tapped them against the table in front of them, and swallowed the liquid down. Ilya smacked a wet, aggressive kiss against his cheek.

“Careful,” Shane cautioned.

“I don’t care unless you do.”

“Buddy—” Ilya mouthed the word after Shane spoke, seemingly amused at his word choice, “This is all at your pace,” he gestured around them. “I don’t give a single fuck about what anyone thinks when it comes to you. You're mine.”

“Oh wow,” breathed Hayden, somewhere that wasn’t directly in front of Shane, which was where Ilya’s face was. Their noses were only like an inch—maybe less, his depth perception was fucked right now—from touching. “He’s super drunk.”

“How can you tell?” Cliff asked.

“No filter,” Hayden and Rose confirmed.

Ilya’s smile grew teeth, but the effort it took for him not to laugh made him look boyish and handsome, rather than whatever roguish, sexy bit he usually tried for in these situations.

“New favorite Hollander, da?”

Shane could feel just how pink his cheeks were.

“Oh my god,” Rose squealed. “Ilya totally—”

“You are maybe right about skipping the dance floor,” he murmured, leaning to the side and nudging Shane’s chin to the left so he could speak into Shane’s ear without the others reading his lips. “I bet you would embarrass yourself in,” he paused for emphasis, “Other ways if I danced with you.” Now the grin grew serious, a hook of heat catching in Shane’s belly and pulling tight.

“I don’t know about that,” Shane said, distantly aware he was about to get goaded into doing something he’d insisted he did not want to do. In his defense, when Ilya described it that way, it didn’t sound so bad. Sure, he’d probably feel some level of horrified about his poor dancing or possibly the exhibitionism Ilya was definitely suggesting, but this was Ilya’s world. Or at least, it had been.

And the last time they’d been at the same club was in the top five worst days of his life. This could never come close to that.

“I am a really bad dancer,” Shane pressed.

“I remember,” Ilya smirked, gaze flicking to Rose and back. “I will make you look like pro.”

“I thought you were going to embarrass me.”

The smirk lost its edge. “That too, a little. We will dance and when you are done we will go back to hotel room. I will do anything you want.”

“You are so fucking whipped, brother.”

Ilya turned away from Shane, though he held his hand out and pulled Shane up with him. He looked down at Cliff with a helpless expression. “This is true,” he agreed, accepting the chirp like an award.

“You’re unbelievable,” Shane said, nudging him with his hip. Only Ilya could make being whipped sound like something to aspire toward. “Come on, let’s get this over with.”

“Nooo, Shane, not ‘get this over with,’” he pouted. “Will be fun. You will forget about everyone but me and we will dance all night long.”

“Two songs, if you’re lucky,” Shane barted.

“At least three!”

Once again, Shane thought unbelievable, even as he yielded, “Maybe.”

Chapter 20

Chapter Notes

As always, I am so grateful you've taken the time out of your day to read.

Chapter count will be going up again. I have a solid 10 chapter buffer right now and just finished writing Chapter 31. I think there's a just a couple chapters to go and will finalize that number once I know for sure.

Anyway, I hope you enjoy this one and have a wonderful week.

PS: shoutout to the one commenter who realized what happened last chapter. 😌

“I drooled on your shoulder,” Shane complained, blinking himself awake to a throbbing headache and the dull roar of a commercial flight. A baby was crying back in economy and the sound rattled around his skull like a pinball pinging between gray matter and bone.

Ilya’s very unhelpful response was a soft snore. His fingers, limply tangled with Shane's and tucked discreetly between their thighs, twitched reflexively as he dreamed.

Smoothing his thumb over the back of Ilya's hand, Shane let his head fall back against Ilya's shoulder, careful to avoid his earlier drool patch. He was grateful for the frequent travel required in his line of work, because it meant he could fall asleep on any method of transportation.

He desperately needed that sleep. They hadn't gotten back to their hotel until nearly five in the morning, which had given them enough time to shower, slug down electrolytes, and get in an uber to LAX. Shane had drunkenly told Ilya that it was like that time he’d won the cup in Colorado back in 2016 and partied all night, going directly from the bar to the airport, except this was better, on account of the company. Ilya had been pointed out how much dancing they'd done, too, which Shane insisted he had been tricked into under duress, while Ilya rubbed their noses together and insisted, ‘you liked it, didn't you?’

It felt like Shane blinked and they were landing in Chicago. Layovers sucked, especially in large cities like Chicago or New York, but Ilya made it easy, grabbing both their bags and bullying Shane to a burger place on the concourse he swore would cure their hangovers way better than a sub-par smoothie full of added sugar from Jamba Juice.

One incredibly greasy burger, a couple stolen tater tots, and ginger ale later, Shane found Ilya was right: he helt decidedly more human. It wouldn't change the fact that he would not be removing his sunglasses and planned to be in bed as soon as they got home and showered off the unpleasant film of travel from their skin, but he didn't feel like his phone screen was going to blind him when he looked at it anymore and that was something.

“There are pictures from the club,” Ilya remarked, sliding his phone across the small table mid scroll. The wrappers from their burgers had been nearly tucked into the bag they'd been delivered in. “None of us dancing, though.”

He sounded disappointed. “Is,” Shane swallowed, considering the concept of ordering more tater tots for the salt alone and steered his brain toward an overpriced packet of liquid IV and bottle of water at whatever Hudson News stall they happened to pass on their way to their gate. “Is that what you wanted to happen?”

Ilya didn’t even try to hide behind a lie. “Is not not what I wanted to happen,” he supposed, and yeah, he was definitely pouting about it. “I know it is stupid, but they are still writing articles about you and Rose, even though you told the world you are in relationship with someone else. Some of them think you are lying because they have not seen your partner.”

“You are literally with me everywhere, Ilya.”

Ilya's eyes narrowed. “Yes, but they don’t know I am with you like that, Shane.”

Shane searched Ilya’s eyes, trying to figure out what the play was. Drunk as he’d been, he hadn’t been kidding last night. If Ilya wanted to go public, Shane was with him. The fact that they hadn’t been caught making out on the dancefloor of the club and photographed from every angle was frankly astonishing. Shane had been too drunk to care or pay attention to anyone who wasn’t Ilya once they got going, but they had been surrounded by people the whole time. Surely someone had recognized them, right?

Then again, it wasn’t like they’d been in Ottawa or Montreal. One of the good things LA had going for it was that nobody really cared about anyone who wasn’t an A-list celebrity, and Canadian hockey players simply didn’t make that cut. Without Rose directly beside them, they were basically anonymous.

Besides: Did Shane really want their relationship made public via sloppy photos taken in some dimly lit club or clambering into the back of an Uber that smelled like weed? Not really, but it wasn’t like there was any way they could go public that wouldn’t make waves in the hockey world. It was another reason Shane had refused to make a plan for it, had offered to do it Ilya’s way. He didn’t want to have expectations.

What was the point of making himself sick trying to control public opinion? He’d already learned that lesson the hard way. People would think what they wanted and it would suck or it wouldn’t, and the world would spin on regardless.

With that in mind, Shane pushed himself up from their shared table, walking their grease-splotched bag of wadded food wrappers and napkins to the nearest receptacle before coming back to grab his rollerbag. “You want them to know?”

Ilya’s face did something complicated, like Shane had dangled something he desperately wanted in front of him but planned to take it away if he reached for it.

It wasn’t the first time Shane had seen this expression, nor the first time something cold and angry settled in his gut because of it. Whoever had taught Ilya to expect something so terrible, Shane didn't know, but he had his suspicions. Privately, he was glad Ilya was no longer exposed to them, regardless of the reason.

Even if they were family.

“I do,” he admitted, carefully. He looked like he wanted to say something else, but didn't allow himself to.

Shane nodded and offered Ilya his hand to pull him up. Ilya took it, letting go when he got to his feet.

“What do you think the chances are of us getting spotted in the Chicago airport?” Shane asked him.

Ilya’s eyebrows drew together in confusion. “I don’t know…” he trailed off, slinging his backpack over one shoulder and reaching for his rollerbag. “There are many bored people waiting for planes. Why?”

“Want to find out?” He held out his hand again, wiggling his fingers.

Ilya eyed them dubiously.

“You want to hold hands in the airport.”

“Well, we’ve done everything but PDA—except last night,” he corrected, because there were some blurry memories of very public, borderline indecent displays of affection in his brain, “And nobody seems to have figured it out. So we either find out if hand holding is a bro activity or they’ll know. Mom says the fandom is pretty sure we’re a thing, which horrifies me because why is she on Tumblr—”

“How do you know what Tumblr is?” Ilya cut in, looking at Shane like he’d never seen him before.

“Derek, by way of Luca,” He held out his hands to declare himself innocent. “Wait, why do you?”

“Not important.” Ilya claimed his left hand, their fingers lacing together on autopilot. Shane allowed Ilya to distract him. “So we just do this and people maybe figure it out?”

“Do you have a better idea—” Ilya perked up, but Shane continued, “That doesn’t involve TSA detaining us for public indecency?”

“I’m sure we could get out of it by signing autographs,” he said, like that was a reasonable way to skirt the law. “Chicago’s hockey team has been shit for years now.”

Shane yanked hard on their combined hands in mock rebuke, taking advantage of Ilya swaying his way to peck his cheek.

Ilya froze.

“Okay?” Shane looked over at him in time to see his surprise give way to delightedness. It was like watching sunshine break through clouds.

“Can we make out at gate?” Ilya begged. “Would be great pre-flight entertainment for the other passengers.”

Rolling his eyes, Shane tugged on their hands to get him to follow along. “That’s gross, Ilya,” he grumbled, but he was smiling too widely to convince Ilya to drop it.


—


That afternoon, five photos had been posted of Shane and Ilya walking between terminals at Chicago O’Hare, their hands linked and swinging idly between them (which Shane recalled because he’d been there, but someone had also recorded a video of it while whispering, holy shit, babe, are you seeing this?) to Twitter.

Ilya was over the moon about it. First and foremost, despite the fact that they were hungover in the way Shane almost exclusively saved for championship wins, they did look very cute together: Ilya in all black, backwards baseball cap on his head to tame his frizzy curls, Shane wearing a red Centaurs hoodie with the team’s name in stylized cursive rather than the team’s criminally ugly logo and the extra soft joggers he typically never wore in public but Ilya had thrown at him when he’d gotten out of the shower that morning because he had been irritated by the hotel’s scratchy towels, both of them in matching sunglasses despite the fact that they were indoors and Chicago O’Hare was dimly lit at best.

Secondly, the video had caught them mid-conversation, Ilya facing away, Shane more visible. They passed perpendicular to whomever recorded the video, Shane huffing before he smiled at whatever Ilya had said—which hadn’t made the video beyond the usual semi-harsh curl of his accent around English. That was probably a good thing. He had probably been begging Shane to make out with him at the gate. He hadn't stopped until they’d gotten on the plane (mostly because he’d fallen asleep while the flight attendants did their safety announcements).

“Alright,” Harris said, ignoring Ilya’s swanning around the locker room on their first practice back from the All Star break because unlike everyone who had muted the group chat to go on vacation or generally enjoy their time off, his job didn’t allow for a break when there were no games and he’d seen the pictures almost as soon as they’d been posted. “I won the coin toss with the PR team so… Shane, Ilya, we need to chat before you go out on the ice.”

Shane was pretty sure this was more of a communications matter anyways. Farah and his mom had also sent the posts to them in a panic because they hadn’t told anyone to expect them to go public at one pm central on a Monday. He was pretty sure they'd also been in touch with Harris, trying to look out for Shane and Ilya's best interests. Optics-wise, it wasn’t exactly the best timing, but Shane was pretty sure there were no best case scenarios when it came to this sort of thing.

Harris’ office was too small for two adults, much less two adult hockey players and Harris, so he led them to Wiebe’s office instead. Ilya cast a weary look up at the lights, but Shane shrugged. He’d been back in the coach’s office a couple times over the season and it was better now. He didn’t associate the yellowy lights with that horrible time in his life as much. A therapist would probably call it growth. Shane’s sport psychologist certainly did.

“Alright,” Harris said again, sitting on Wiebe’s desk instead of in the man’s desk chair. “Off the record—” he made a shrill sound. “Very exciting—unless this was an accident and we’re not—” They both shook their heads, and Harris grinned. “Thank fuck,” he breathed, then clapped his hands. “Okay, back to business: Are we making a statement or…” He looked between them, but mostly at Shane, hands spread to indicate he needed them to fill in the blank.

Shane looked to Ilya. “Your call.”

It wasn’t like most people who’d followed the sport for the last few years couldn’t put two and two together. Technically they didn’t have to say anything.

Still, Ilya chewed his lip. “I want to make statement, I think. Probably.”

Probably my ass, Shane thought. Ilya hadn’t been particularly stealthy about the drafts in his notes app, which he’d been working on since landing in YOW and learning that photos had hit the internet while taxiing to their gate. They'd discussed it in the car on the way home, trying to find common ground between Shane's belief that no straight players had to confirm their relationships so why should they and Ilya's need to be seen as more than a pseudonym on Shane's hockey stick.

“Nothing formal, though,” Ilya insisted. “I can just tell media after practice or game, yes?”

“You could post on socials too,” Harris helpfully pointed out. “An instagram post seems formal these days, but it really isn’t. Then you wouldn’t have to answer a bunch of questions.”

Ilya blew a raspberry in Harris’ direction. “You forget who you are talking to. I want to answer all their questions. I want to brag about how sexy and beautiful my boyfriend is—”

Shane gave him a look.

“Within reason, of course,” he corrected.

“Right,” Harris said, with a relieved glance in Shane’s direction, not that Shane could actually control Ilya like the people who knew about their relationship seemed to think he could. “Carlton noted that there’d probably be some blowback from the powers that be, so expect him to meet with you after practice today. Nobody’s in trouble and he’s not mad because you disclosed it to him—thank you for doing that, by the way, I just about had a heart attack when I found out about the pictures, but management said they were shocked you held out this long.”

“I think we're more shocked that the masses didn't figure it out,” Shane said. “We've been seen everywhere together.”

“You give straight men too much credit,” Harris commented dryly. Ilya nodded along like this was sage wisdom every queer person knew, and yeah, maybe it was. “They could see you kiss—with tongue—and find a way to explain it away as some kind of dude-bro masculinity thing.”

“Ugh,” Shane muttered.


—


That evening, Ilya tagged Shane in an Instagram post like Harris suggested.

It made Shane feel a little antsy and unsettled not to be in control, even though it was what he'd wanted: at least, in that he wanted Ilya to do this for himself, on his terms. Loving Ilya, knowing that he was and would always be Shane's person, meant accepting the chaos he came with, including the excessive sharing that was Shane's very antithesis.

The lineup of photos wasn't as cliched as Shane expected, though he suspected that was to do with the fact that the vast majority of photos of them prior to 2021 included Shane in a Metros sweater.

The first was a picture of them from Christmas, arms wrapped around each other by the fireplace at his parents’ house, which Shane distinctly remembered his mom snapping so she could add it to the photo wall in the hallway. Next, a candid Hayden had taken of them arguing about something over American Thanksgiving, their noses nearly touching, followed by an obligatory photo of them facing off against each other, but in practice: Shane in a white sweater, Ilya in black and red. After that, a photo Ilya had sent Hayden last May, their hands grasping each other tightly, coffees slightly out of focus in the dim light of the cafe.

The post itself had no caption, but then again, it didn't need one.

The last photo in the carousel was a screenshot of their Instagram messages, Ilya's truth laid bare for the whole world to see.

Shane blinked tears down his cheeks when he saw it.

The first time he'd seen those messages, just over a year ago now, the entire world had shifted on its axis. Ilya's words had told him there was still something to fight for, so he’d made a plan and been single-minded in its execution, his return. They'd been an eye opener, told him he'd been wrong when he thought he was waiting for his life to begin because it was already fucking happening without him.

It still hurt his heart to look at them. Shane had come so close to losing Ilya without ever acknowledging this thing between them, this love that Shane would not compromise for.

That was why he knew he wouldn't be able to wait. Maybe it was too fast or too soon, and it definitely wasn't to plan—because he’d wanted to for this. He'd had ideas about the cottage, firelight casting a golden glow over Ilya's cheeks, the summer sky an inky smudge reflected in the lake, Shane pretending to go get them more beers from inside when he was actually retrieving the ring he’d collected from the jeweler with Rose and Svetlana after the All-Star Game from its still to be determined hiding place—

Conceptually, it was a nice idea. Perfect, even.

Except it was never going to be perfect, and neither was he. All he could do was be honest. With himself, and with Ilya. That was what mattered.

And truthfully? Shane had learned his lesson.

He was done waiting for his life to happen to him.

Chapter 21

February, 2022
Ottawa, ON

Ilya patted his chest when one of Harris’ assistants grabbed him after morning skate, fingers sliding over the smooth moisture-wicking material of his club hoodie. Shane watched from afar as his eyes crinkled at the corners when he felt the outline of his necklace beneath the material, his expression softening momentarily before Slavic composure replaced it.

This, Ilya had said the morning after his Instagram post had all but broken the hockey-loving corner of the internet and compelled Shane to a rare act of spontaneity, wasn’t for the world, but for them.

It wasn’t about secrets, but rather getting to experience joy without outsiders trying to capitalize on it for their own gains.

Shane waited for Ilya down the hall, just outside the doors to the Centaurs’ press room. Carlton was already addressing the media with a quick statement on behalf of the owners. He would be staying to moderate the media’s questions for Shane and Ilya afterward.

“Ready?”

“Ready,” Ilya confirmed. His hair was still wet. “I am surprised they did not want us in suits.”

“It’s not a trial.”

Ilya muttered a terse, “Not yet,” under his breath. He looked like he was marching to his execution.

Shane couldn’t blame him for the feeling. He might have shared it if not for the events of the year previous.

The League was unhappy with them. It didn't take a genius to figure out why.

Even this semi-new regime couldn’t necessarily flip from its exceedingly conservative values just because there had been legal trouble the season before. Carlton, the Centaurs’ General Manager, had been quick to explain that not every owner was as morally sound as the siblings who owned the Cents after they revealed their relationship to Wiebe in October.

The League tolerated Shane being gay because legally they had no choice, and Scott Hunter had doubled down on that decision in an incredibly public way. The handful of other players who had quietly come after that were nothing by comparison, barely blips on their radar. In terms of professional sports, only hardcore fans followed individual players who weren't franchise superstars.

It made Shane and Ilya stick out that much more. A single, high profile queer player was one thing, but two queer players, in a relationship, on the same team? The League felt it was a major issue, one potentially requiring intervention.

Shane was grateful that Derek and Luca evaded scrutiny mostly by way of plausible deniability and the long shadows cast by their mentors. The owners around the league were more concerned with the veteran players with a storied past, whom the League had consistently billed as rivals since the moment their paths crossed more than a decade earlier. Maybe set apart, it wouldn’t be an issue if they were both queer.

The problem was that the top two players in the league—of a generation, really—were queer together. Apparently that was enough for the League to make accusations.

Thus: Here they stood, about to be paraded in front of the media not to share their joy, but to defend themselves. So much for avoiding a press conference and keeping it to the usual media scrums.

“I’m not worried,” Shane said.

“Mm, no, of course not,” Ilya grinned. “You have good lawyers.”

“We have good lawyers,” he corrected. “And Farah.”

“And Farah,” Ilya agreed.

Shane was just glad Ilya had decided to bring her on as his agent at the beginning of the season. Every action they took would be held under a microscope for the foreseeable future, and it was nice to know they had the same point of contact for things.

A moment later they were marched out to the flashbang of cameras, which was unpleasant but expected, and walked along the narrow, raised platform to the seats beside the podium where Carlton had turned to greet them. He shook each of their hands, his expression neutral, but his eyes kind.

Carlton was a guy who loved the game but had never played, a stats nerd with an eye for the game who wasn’t afraid to be creative about sourcing players. With them, he was genuine if a little awkward, and while a lot of teams positioned their general manager as the big bad wolf, the guy who only called when you fucked up or if he planned to trade you, Carlton was like the rest of the Centaurs’ organization in that he was way more present. He met with his players often to make sure he had his finger on the pulse of the team and its needs.

“Ilya and Shane have graciously agreed to answer your questions,” Carlton said when they sat down in their respective rickety, yet padded folding chairs. Ilya sprawled in a slouch while Shane kept his spine perfectly straight. “I’ll be here to moderate and direct traffic.”

Leaning forward, Ilya fiddled with the base of the little stand mic. Shane looked over at him, but he shook his head.

“Gentlemen, are you ready to get started?”

They both nodded. A massive camera in the back of the room swung toward them as its operator zoomed in.

“Let’s go Trace first, then Angelo, unless…” Carlton looked over at them briefly.

Shane nodded sharply. Best to cut them off at the pass, as his dad liked to say.

Under the table, hidden from curious eyes, Ilya’s hand found his and squeezed before it skated up his thigh and disengaged.

Before, that sort of thing might have been panic inducing, but all Shane felt was calm. Settled. He didn't like going up in front of the media, but knowing that Ilya loved him, knowing he wanted forever with Shane, well, it kind of made Shane feel like he could run through a wall. He leaned forward, toward the microphone.

“Um,” he began, inelegantly, “Obviously all of you are aware that the League announced that they’re investigating Ilya and me, as well as the Ottawa Centaurs Organization. We've spoken to Centaurs leadership, the PA, and our agent and fully intend to comply with their investigation.”

He looked over at Ilya, who was gazing into the middle distance with that angry-looking gaze that meant he was trying not to let real emotion show. He nodded dispassionately.

“Thank you, Shane,” Carlton said. “Trace, you're up.”

They looked around the room for the short man from the Athletic. Shane knew him for obvious reasons, but Ilya hadn't seen him before—he was a former Montreal reporter who now worked out of Vancouver.

“Trace McDonagh with the Athletic,” he announced himself into the wireless microphone handed to him by the team’s staffer. “For Ilya,” he began, when Ilya’s eyes landed on him. “You’ve been the captain of this team for four seasons now. How do you feel about the accusation that you and your team engaged in tampering to secure a deal with Shane?”

Ilya’s knee nudged Shane's under the table. He’d been slouching back but he leaned forward, their postures matching. “They are entitled to due diligence, but I think it is stupid,” he said flatly. “The Centaurs have built good organization. Strong culture, strong team. They do not need to cheat to convince players to sign.”

Shane nodded his agreement. It was strange to see Ilya so serious in the public eye. It was clear he thought the whole thing was bullshit, but he took his role with the team seriously. Shane was the hometown boy, but Ilya was the face of this franchise: its captain and all time top performer.

Satisfied, Trace handed the microphone off to one of the Centaurs’ PR people who ducked as they ran down the aisle, mostly out of the camera’s view. A man two rows up received the microphone next.

“Angelo Williams, Ottawa Herald,” he introduced himself. “For both of you: What was your reaction to the League's concerns about match fixing? You've been considered rivals since before the draft, and the Montreal-Boston rivalry has been ongoing for decades.”

“Ilya?”

“You go,” Ilya deferred sharply.

Shane shrugged. “To quote Ilya’s earlier response, ‘it’s stupid.’”

There was a quiet exhale, a silent laugh from reporters. Ilya sniffed just loud enough for his mic to pick it up. The room waited for Shane to continue.

“The thing about Ilya, the reason he's so well regarded in every locker room and team he's played for is because he has integrity.” He surveyed the room, front teeth pressing into his lower lip. “He’s an asshole, and I'll be the first to say it, but more than that he's a good captain and an even better person.”

Ilya kicked him under the table. When Shane looked over at him, his head was turned away.

“Both of us have always strived to bring our best to every game we play,” Shane continued. “I'll admit that it's easier to do when the competition is fiercer, and there's nobody I wanted to beat more than Ilya when we were on opposing teams. Even though I'll never forgive him for it, there's a reason he went first in our draft year. And I think,” he scratched his chin, “Everybody in this room can see it.”

They did, Shane knew. Ilya's number would hang from the fucking rafters here and in Boston, if not everywhere else.

He licked his lips. “So I guess, to answer your question, I'm angry that the League would even consider that to be a possibility. We both led our respective teams to cups in the 2010s, and regardless of what we were or weren't to each other at that time, it's an insult to think that either of us would give anything less than our all. We wouldn’t be sitting here talking about this if we were siblings who’d played on rival teams.”

Ilya's chair scratched beside Shane. “If I were able to decide games by myself, Ottawa would have made playoffs before last season,” he said dryly before turning to Carlton. “No offense.”

Mostly amused, Carlton shook his head and leaned in toward his mic. “Philip Dupont.”

“For Ilya, please,” Philip murmured with a Quebecois accent. “How has this—being outed, or coming out, I’m not sure how you’d put it,” Ilya snorted just inside the mic’s range as the man finished, “Impacted your relationship with your home country?”

Ilya’s sigh was heavy. They’d both expected this question would come up. If anything it was surprising this hadn’t been the first thing, rather than the investigation itself.

“Russia is Russia,” he said carefully. The topic was tenuous at best, and Ilya treated it seriously. “I have not heard anything but their stand—stance,” he corrected, “On homosexuality is not positive. I have been in North America many years, yes? Ottawa is my home now.”

He finished with a glance to Carlton and received a microscopic nod. Less was more with the Russia situation. Getting into citizenship and residency would only provoke Russia to make an example out of him.

“Thank you.”

Ilya nodded. Carlton moved on. “Darren.”

Darren Middleton, a tall, lanky man with bright red hair and a black jacket with the ESPN logo reached past nearly three people to take the mic from the staffer in the aisle. His wingspan was weirdly impressive. “Hi guys,” he said, in a raspy voice, “This one’s for Shane.”

Shane nodded.

“The last year has had a lot of ups and downs for you in terms of your relationship with the league. Could you expand upon that?”

“I mean,” Ilya’s foot tapped against Shane’s gently, giving him pause.

“I think everyone knows that Shane Hollander has had difficult relationship with MLH,” Ilya interjected.

“Fair,” Middleton agreed. The reporter squared his shoulders. “I mean this in terms of,” he looked back to Shane, “Trust. Do you trust that the League has your best interests at heart?”

Shane didn’t let himself look at Ilya, who shifted in his periphery, and he resisted the urge to shrug lest someone try and do some weird deep-dive analysis about his body language. Shane had tried trusting the league once and look what had happened. The League only had its own best interests at heart, but Shane could hardly voice that thought verbatim.

“I don’t think trust enters into it,” he answered, as close to honest as he dared to be. “The League is taking action to ensure that my actions are above board. They’re entitled to their due diligence.”

The reporter’s lips parted, likely about to ask a follow up but Carlton seamlessly intervened.

“Let’s give others a turn, please,” he said placidly.

And really, what could Shane say if asked point blank about how he felt or reacted? That he and Ilya had been on a Zoom call together with their agent and the League representative while they gave their bullshit summation of ‘we’re investigating your relationship because we think it’s wrong and this is the only possible way we can get rid of you without ruining our reputation’ and Ilya’s eyes had gone wide as saucers in disbelief that they wanted to investigate everything from 2010 to now while Shane mentally scoffed and thought about just how much money in jersey sales they’d made off him and especially Ilya, not to mention if the Board of Governors actually fucking realized just what they were authorizing the League to do.

Shane’s real answer was actually that he’d nodded when they asked for compliance and told them that any further discussion would happen with Farah and their lawyers present, then disconnected them from the Zoom call while Ilya threw every throw pillow from his side of the couch at the wall instead of Shane’s iPad. His on the record response to any further questions would have been something along the lines of ‘Like I said earlier, we told them we’ll comply with their investigation,’ followed by a load bearing ‘no comment’ to whatever bullshit they were asked next.

Ultimately, Carlton or not, the whole thing was bound to run away from them eventually. The media had a job to do, and it wasn’t always one Shane appreciated. Besides, Shane and Ilya were technically employees. Well paid ones, obviously, but they worked for the MLH and were still beholden to its wishes, which included being made available to the media.

While he’d been lost in thought, the mic went to Davey, one of the regulars in the Centaurs locker room. He, at very least, started with levity. “Been a pretty hectic week for you guys, eh?”

“Maybe a little,” Ilya allowed, bracing his chin with a palm, elbow resting on the table. “Good thing for you, yes?”

Davey smiled at them. “Maybe a little,” he repeated, a little cheeky.

Ilya shook his head, still in his palm, and huffed. “Ask your question, Davey, we would like to go home and nap before game tonight.”

“Fair,” he said. “Shane, I’d like you to take us through the last week. First you’re fined at the All Star Game, then you’re photographed in clubs and airports across the US, your relationship with Ilya is made public, and now this. You’ve always been reserved with the media, even before the events that transpired last spring. A lot of folks are reporting that you’re in your bad boy era. Can you maybe speak to that, about how the last while has been for you in terms of perception?”

Wow. That was a question, Shane thought, kind of actually furious about it. He understood why Davey was asking, but wondered if he knew just how much good will he stood to lose. Ilya was practically vibrating beside him, ready to jump to his defense.

It wouldn’t be useful, Shane thought, so he stepped pointedly on the toes of Ilya’s Jordans to redirect his anger and attention.

“Well,” he exhaled slowly, forcing himself to keep his tone light, “At least you summarized the last week so I don’t have to.”

There was another smattering of laughter, which bought him time to collect his thoughts. Even though Ilya always spoke highly of this reporter, he still had a job to do, which was investigative journalism, even if he was just investigating the lives and performances of hockey players. He wasn’t their friend, no matter how friendly or good of a person he was.

“I wouldn’t say I’m in my ‘bad boy era’ or whatever,” he airquoted, “I’m just being myself. I’m not perfect. I don’t always have a perfect answer to give,” he gestured to himself as if indicating his current response. “And in terms of perception,” he pressed his lips together. “I want to be known as a good hockey player and a good person. Sometimes I mess up: I left the locker room before media wrapped at All Stars and got fined. That’s on me. That night I went out to a club and there were pictures taken. The next day I was traveling home with my partner and more pictures were taken.” He allowed himself a long overdue shrug. “I’m not the only guy who does these things, I’m just the guy people are talking about because my partner is a man who’s also my teammate.”

He looked over at Ilya, who instantly sensed his gaze. Their eyes met. There was just something about being here with him, even if they were barely touching, that just… made him want to be brave. He let his eyes flutter closed, took a deep breath. Opened them again. Ilya gave him a barely perceptible nod, a little check in.

“I think,” Ilya added before Shane could continue, “People think the access they are given to public figures means they know them as person. Is not true. We are people just like anyone else, we are just good at hockey and you want us to bring home championship for you.” He frowned, “Or you should. Because we are going to.”

“Agreed,” Shane took over. “When Ilya and I told the organization we were together, Ilya told them that we don’t owe anyone else our truth.” He noticed, out of the corner of his eye that Ilya’s thumb was stroking at the added weight on his necklace. “I don’t think that makes me a villain, but if someone does think that,” he shook his head microscopically, lips pursed together as he processed how to finish his statement, “Fine.”

The next question was asked by an insider who more or less worked for the team. A softball question: “You were the top anticipated free agent this summer. Were you always going to play here or were there other contenders?”

Even though he’d been asked it practically a hundred times, Shane looked at the table for a moment to collect his thoughts, just in case. “Ottawa’s home. I grew up here. If I’m being honest, there were only two teams in the League I ever wanted to play for: the one that drafted me, and the one I grew up cheering for. The first option isn’t available to me, and I was ready to come home.”

One of their other regulars, from some podcast Ilya insisted was hoaky and cliched but very fun, asked if they expected to be benched.

They hadn’t been told they would be and Shane really fucking hoped not.

“Last question,” Carlton called out. “Shae Woods.”

The woman at the back of the room had an inviting smile that was a touch too genuine for the average reporter. “Shae Woods, TSN,” she introduced herself. “For both Shane and Ilya: To piggyback off Shane’s earlier answer, your relationship is new to a lot of folks who only watch men’s professional sports. Female athletes have been out and played with each other in soccer and basketball, even hockey. Do you feel like the league could take note of this? It’s hardly a new precedent.”

“Absolutely,” Shane agreed. “I think the thing people don’t want to acknowledge is that queer people have always been in sport, but yes. There’s definitely precedent in women’s athletics.”

She nodded, looking amused at his emphatic answer. Just as quickly, she tipped her head to the side to regard Ilya.

“Shae Woods, yes?”

Ilya pronounced her name carefully, hand clamped over Shane’s knee under the table before sliding up, his fingers toying with the coarse hair above the joint as the reporter nodded. Ilya swallowed, lips thinning as he thought through how he wanted to respond.

“The League should, how you say, take note,” the words came out harsher in his accent, the consonants harder. His eyes narrowed, roving over the faces of the reporters facing them with their hand held devices before resting on the camera.

“I think MLH and Players Association should consider better resources for queer players—whether they are out or not. Women's sports does this. Men like to think they are better than women and yet women take far better care of each other than men do.” He paused. “By the way, I am big fan. You wrote great article about equal pay in women’s sport last year. I did not read it,” some of the reporters laughed, but Ilya glared at them until they realized he wasn’t making a joke. “But I listened to you discuss it with Meaghan and Tessa on Cleats and Curls podcast last year. Was great episode. Should be talked about more.”

People tended to forget that Ilya, despite his actual reputation as a bad boy and his assholish exterior on the ice, was incredibly intelligent. He pretended otherwise, but for a man who acted like reading was his enemy and would rather sleep through a documentary, he listened to a lot of podcasts. He said it helped him with his English, especially in those first few years in North America and then it had just been a thing he did. Sometimes, he’d put a podcast on while they were home and they’d talk about it after. It was possibly one of Shane’s favorite couple things that they did (aside from the obvious).

Idly, Shane wondered just how many of these reporters would leave here surprised at Ilya’s well-informed feelings on the subject.

Shae blinked, but recovered quickly enough. “Wow. Thank you.”

“Thank you,” Ilya returned. “Is important to talk about these things.”

She shrugged. “That’s why I’m here. To talk about important issues and hold people accountable.”

Carlton stopped the conversation there. He thanked Ilya, Shane, and the reporters for their time, and then it was over.

As they were led away, Ilya looked over his shoulder at the departing reporters and murmured, “If we ever decide to do big interview, I want it to be with her. Fuck the rest of them.”

Shane snorted. “Fine by me,” he agreed, though in that moment he’d have been fine with never doing media ever again.

Chapter 22

The investigation itself was a farce. Shane knew it, Ilya knew it, their agent and lawyers knew it, but it was allowed through several very weak contractual clauses and something that they ultimately had to deal with by nature of being the first out couple in men’s professional hockey. They didn’t get benched, but they were interviewed by league officials with their lawyers and Farah present, first together, then separately, then together again.

They were asked about random games Ilya didn’t remember but Shane did because he had a mind for that sort of thing, were shown game footage of penalties and faceoffs and asked but why did you do that and do you remember what you said here, meanwhile their representation argued, how would they possibly remember what they said to one another on some random Tuesday in April, 2013, and Shane would say, Ilya told me he saw that one cologne commercial I had, that I must really like wearing makeup and walking around without a shirt, did my mom think I looked pretty? He wanted to distract me so he’d win the faceoff.

The whole thing was offensive: they were grown men, they’d only played each other fourteen times in the playoffs over seven years, and the league truly had nothing on them other than a disdain for queer players—they tried for homosexual but Shane had insisted they use the word queer because he’d been reading up on the concept of bi-erasure.

Worst of all, it was just… uncomfortably invasive.

They didn’t owe anybody the details of how long it’d been since they’d first had relations, much less how long they’d been in a relationship, how they defined it. They certainly didn’t owe it to the League, who would just as soon slap progress flags over their shoulders and pretend like they’d always been LGBTQ+ friendly if they thought it would make them more money.

Shane was, not for the first time, glad he’d separated his love of hockey from the league he played in. The league was a business, and its players were independent contractors. That mindset helped him. His past experiences with MLH leadership likewise helped. He knew to expect the worst, and come prepared, even though anyone not blinded by bigotry or ignorance could tell their investigation would only prove that queer players could absolutely be the best in sport.

So yeah, even though it was more or less wrapped in ten days, the investigation deemed resolved like the League could just sweep it under the rug and pretend like they’d never said anything, it was a rough start to February. Especially for Ilya.

Anybody would struggle with that level of scrutiny. While Ilya had come out on his own terms, and there were a lot of good things happening in their personal lives, the news was public.
